|\mathsf{A}|_{M,\varphi}:=\sum_{m=0}^{M}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1},\dots,a_{m}\\
b_{1},\dots,b_{k}\\
c_{1},\dots,c_{l}\end{subarray}}|\partial^{m}_{a_{1}\dots a_{m}}\mathsf{A}^{b_{1}\dots b_{k}}_{c_{1}\dots c_{l}}|\text{.}

Definition 2.7
.

Let
\mathscr{M}
be an aAdS region, let
M\geq 0
, and let
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathfrak{A}
be a vertical tensor field and a tensor field on
\mathscr{I}
, respectively, both of the same rank
(k,l)
.

•

\mathsf{A}
is
locally bounded in
C^{M}
iff for any compact coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
,

(2.5)

\sup_{(0,\rho_{0}]\times U}|\mathsf{A}|_{M,\varphi}<\infty\text{.}

•

\mathsf{A}

converges to
\mathfrak{A}
in
C^{M}
, denoted
\mathsf{A}\rightarrow^{M}\mathfrak{A}
, iff for any compact coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
,

(2.6)

\lim_{\sigma\searrow 0}\sup_{\{\sigma\}\times U}|\mathsf{A}-\mathfrak{A}|_{M,\varphi}=0\text{.}

We now describe the metrics that we will consider on our aAdS segments.
This is summarized through the notion of “FG-aAdS segments” from
[
45
,
50
]
.

Definition 2.8
.

(\mathscr{M},g)
is called an
FG-aAdS segment
iff the following hold:
35
35

35

Though we refer to
(\mathscr{M},g)
as the FG-aAdS segment, this also implicitly includes the quantities
\mathsf{g}
and
\mathfrak{g}
below.

•

\mathscr{M}
is an aAdS region, and
g
is a Lorentzian metric on
\mathscr{M}
.

•

There exist a vertical tensor field
\mathsf{g}
of rank
(0,2)
and a Lorentzian metric
\mathfrak{g}
on
\mathscr{I}
with

(2.7)

g:=\rho^{-2}(d\rho^{2}+\mathsf{g})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{g}\rightarrow^{0}\mathfrak{g}\text{.}

Remark 2.9
.

Given an FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{M},g)
:

•

We refer to the form (
2.7
) of
g
as the
Fefferman–Graham
(or
FG
)
gauge condition
.

•

We refer to
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g})
as the
conformal boundary
for
(\mathscr{M},g,\rho)
.

The following definitions describe the basic geometric objects in our setting:

Definition 2.10
.

Given an FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{M},g)
:

•

Let
g^{-1}
,
\nabla
,
\nabla^{\sharp}
, and
R
denote the metric dual, the Levi-Civita connection, the gradient, and the Riemann curvature (respectively) associated with the spacetime metric
g
.

•

Let
\mathfrak{g}^{-1}
,
\mathfrak{D}
,
\mathfrak{D}^{\sharp}
, and
\mathfrak{R}
denote the metric dual, the Levi-Civita connection, the gradient, and the Riemann curvature (respectively) associated with the boundary metric
\mathfrak{g}
.

•

Let
\mathsf{g}^{-1}
,
\mathsf{D}
,
\mathsf{D}^{\sharp}
, and
\mathsf{R}
denote the metric dual, the Levi-Civita connection, the gradient, and the Riemann curvature (respectively) associated with the vertical metric
\mathsf{g}
.
36
36

36

More specifically,
\mathsf{g}^{-1}|_{\sigma}
and
\mathsf{R}|_{\sigma}
are the metric dual and Riemann curvature of
\mathsf{g}|_{\sigma}
for any
\sigma\in(0,\rho_{0}]
.
Moreover,
\mathsf{D}
and
\mathsf{D}^{\sharp}
act like the Levi-Civita connection and the gradient for
\mathsf{g}|_{\sigma}
on each
\{\rho=\sigma\}
, for all
\sigma\in(0,\rho_{0}]
.

As is standard, we omit the superscript “
-1
” when describing metric duals in index notion.

Definition 2.11
.

Furthermore, given an FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{M},g)
:

•

Let
W
,
Rc
, and
Rs
denote the Weyl, Ricci, and scalar curvatures (respectively) for
g
.

•

Let
\mathfrak{Rc}
and
\mathfrak{Rs}
denote the Ricci and scalar curvatures (respectively) for
\mathfrak{g}
.

2.2.
Vacuum Spacetimes

The final assumption we will pose is that our spacetime satisfies the Einstein-vacuum equations (with normalized negative cosmological constant).

Definition 2.12
.

An FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{M},g)
is called
vacuum
iff the following holds:

(2.8)

Rc-\frac{1}{2}Rs\cdot g+\Lambda\cdot g=0\text{,}\qquad\Lambda:=-\frac{n(n-1)}{2}\text{.}

Proposition 2.13
.

Suppose
(\mathscr{M},g)
is a vacuum FG-aAdS segment.
Then,

(2.9)

Rc=-n\cdot g\text{,}\qquad Rs=-n(n+1)\text{.}

Furthermore, the following holds with respect to any coordinates on
\mathscr{M}
:

(2.10)

W_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}=R_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}+g_{\alpha\gamma}g_{\beta\delta}-g_{\alpha\delta}g_{\beta\gamma}\text{.}

Proof.

These are direct computations; see
[
50
, Proposition 2.24]
.
∎

The following results, which give partial Fefferman–Graham expansions for Einstein-vacuum metrics from the conformal boundary—are a portion of the main results of
[
50
]
:

Definition 2.14
.

Fix an integer
M\geq 0
.
An FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{M},g)
is
regular to order
M
iff:

•

\mathsf{g}
is locally bounded in
C^{M+2}
.

•

The following holds for any compact coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
:

(2.11)

\sup_{U}\int_{0}^{\rho_{0}}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}|_{0,\varphi}|_{\sigma}d\sigma<\infty\text{.}

Definition 2.15
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a FG-aAdS segment, and let
k\geq 2
.
We say that a tensor field
\mathfrak{A}
on
\mathscr{I}

depends only on
\mathfrak{g}
to order
k
iff
\mathfrak{A}
can be expressed as contractions and tensor products of zero or more instances of each of the following:
\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{-1},\mathfrak{R},\dots,\mathfrak{D}^{k-2}\mathfrak{R}
.

Theorem 2.16
.

[
50
, Theorem 3.3]

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment, and assume
n>1
.
Moreover, suppose
(\mathscr{M},g)
is regular to some order
M\geq n+2
.
Then:

•

\mathsf{g}
and
\mathsf{g}^{-1}
satisfy

(2.12)

\mathsf{g}\rightarrow^{M}\mathfrak{g}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{g}^{-1}\rightarrow^{M}\mathfrak{g}^{-1}\text{.}

•

There exist tensor fields
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}
,
0\leq k<n
, on
\mathscr{I}
such that

(2.13)

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{k}\mathsf{g}\rightarrow^{M-k}k!\,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}\text{,}\qquad\rho\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{k+1}\mathsf{g}\rightarrow^{M-k}0\text{.}

Furthermore,
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}=\mathfrak{g}
, and the following properties hold:

–

If
1\leq k<n
is odd, then
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}=0
.

–

If
2\leq k<n
, then
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}
depends only on
\mathfrak{g}
to order
k
.
In particular,

(2.14)

\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}=-\frac{1}{n-2}\left[\mathfrak{Rc}-\frac{1}{2(n-1)}\mathfrak{Rs}\cdot\mathfrak{g}\right]\text{,}\qquad n>2\text{.}

•

There exists a tensor field
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}
on
\mathscr{I}
such that

(2.15)

\rho\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{n+1}\mathsf{g}\rightarrow^{M-n}n!\,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}\text{.}

In addition,
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}
satisfies the following:

–

Both the
\mathfrak{g}
-trace and the
\mathfrak{g}
-divergence of
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}
vanish on
\mathscr{I}
.

–

If
n
is odd or if
n=2
, then
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}=0
.

–

If
n
is even, then
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}
depends only on
\mathfrak{g}
to order
n
.

•

There exist a
C^{M-n}
tensor field
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\dagger)}
on
\mathscr{I}
such that

(2.16)

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{n}\mathsf{g}-n!\,(\log\rho)\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}\rightarrow^{M-n}n!\,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\dagger)}\text{.}

Remark 2.17
.

In particular, when
n>2
, Theorem
2.16
implies that any vacuum FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{M},g)
is also a
strongly FG-aAdS segment
, in the sense of
[
45
, Definition 2.13]
—that is,

(2.17)

\mathsf{g}\rightarrow^{3}\mathfrak{g}\text{,}\qquad\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}\rightarrow^{2}0\text{,}\qquad\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{2}\mathsf{g}\rightarrow^{1}\bar{\mathfrak{g}}\text{,}

for some rank
(0,2)
tensor field
\bar{\mathfrak{g}}
on
\mathscr{I}
, and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{3}\mathsf{g}
is locally bounded in
C^{0}
.
37
37

37

Notice also that when
n=2
, the conclusions of Theorem
2.16
still imply the limits (
2.17
).

These are the main regularity and asymptotic assumptions required for the Carleman estimates of
[
45
]
to hold.

Corollary 2.18
.

[
50
, Theorem 3.6]

Assume the hypotheses of Theorem
2.16
, and let the quantities
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\dots,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}
be as in the conclusions of Theorem
2.16
.
Then, there exists a
C^{M-n}
tensor field
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
on
\mathscr{I}
and a vertical tensor field
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}
such that the following partial expansion holds for
\mathsf{g}
,

(2.18)

\mathsf{g}=\begin{cases}\sum_{k=0}^{\frac{n-1}{2}}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2k)}\rho^{2k}+\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}\rho^{n}+\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\rho^{n}&\text{$n$ odd,}\\
\sum_{k=0}^{\frac{n-2}{2}}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2k)}\rho^{2k}+\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}\rho^{n}\log\rho+\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}\rho^{n}+\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\rho^{n}&\text{$n$ even,}\end{cases}

where the remainder
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}
satisfies

(2.19)

\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\rightarrow^{M-n}0\text{.}

Furthermore,
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
satisfies the following:

•

If
n
is odd, then the
\mathfrak{g}
-trace and
\mathfrak{g}
-divergence of
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
vanish on
\mathscr{I}
.

•

On the other hand, if
n
is even, then the
\mathfrak{g}
-trace of
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
depends only on
\mathfrak{g}
to order
n
, and the
\mathfrak{g}
-divergence of
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
depend only on
\mathfrak{g}
to order
n+1
.

Remark 2.19
.

The conclusions of Theorem
2.16
and Corollary
2.18
imply that the coefficients
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(1)},\dots,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}
—as well as the
\mathfrak{g}
-trace and the
\mathfrak{g}
-divergence of
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
—are determined by the boundary metric
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
.
As a result, we can view
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
and the
\mathfrak{g}
-trace-free,
\mathfrak{g}
-divergence-free part of
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
as the “free” data for the Einstein-vacuum equations at the conformal boundary.

Definition 2.20
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment, and let
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\dots,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n-1)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
be defined as in the statements of Theorem
2.16
and Corollary
2.18
above.
We then refer to the triple
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
as the
holographic data
associated to or induced by
(\mathscr{M},g)
.

2.3.
The Mixed Covariant Formalism

In this subsection, we recall the notion of mixed tensor fields from
[
45
]
.
In order to better handle some of the more complicated tensorial expressions in this section, we will make use of the following notational conventions for multi-indices:

Definition 2.21
.

In general, we will use symbols containing an overhead bar to denote multi-indices.
Moreover, given a multi-index
\bar{A}:=A_{1}\dots A_{l}
(with spacetime or vertical components):

•

For any
1\leq i\leq l
, we write
\hat{A}_{i}
to denote
\bar{A}
, but with
A_{i}
removed.
Moreover, given another index
B
, we write
\hat{A}_{i}\![B]
to denote
\bar{A}
, but with
A_{i}
replaced by
B
.

•

Similarly, given any
1\leq i,j\leq l
, with
i\neq j
, we write
\hat{A}_{i,j}
to denote
\bar{A}
except with
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
removed.
Furthermore, given any indices
B
and
C
, we write
\hat{A}_{i,j}\![B,C]
to denote
\bar{A}
, but with
A_{i}
and
A_{j}
replaced by
B
and
C
, respectively.

The first step in this process is to construct connections on the vertical bundles.
The Levi-Civita connections
\mathsf{D}
already define covariant derivatives of vertical tensor fields in the vertical directions.
We now extend these connections to also act in the
\rho
-direction.

Proposition 2.22
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment.
There exists a (unique) family of connections
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
on the vertical bundles
\mathsf{V}^{k}_{l}\mathscr{M}
, for all ranks
(k,l)
, such that given any vertical tensor field
\mathsf{A}
of rank
(k,l)
, the following formula holds, with respect to any coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
,

(2.20)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}c}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cd}\,\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![d]}{}_{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{b_{j}c}\,\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![d]}\text{,}

where
\bar{a}:=a_{1}\dots a_{k}
and
\bar{b}:=b_{1}\dots b_{l}
are multi-indices.

Furthermore, given any vector field on
X
on
\mathscr{M}
, the operator
\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}
satisfies the following:

•

For any vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{A}
and
\mathsf{B}
,

(2.21)

\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}(\mathsf{A}\otimes\mathsf{B})=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}\mathsf{A}\otimes\mathsf{B}+\mathsf{A}\otimes\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}\mathsf{B}\text{.}

•

For any vertical tensor field
\mathsf{A}
and any tensor contraction operation
\mathcal{C}
,

(2.22)

\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}(\mathcal{C}\mathsf{A})=\mathcal{C}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}\mathsf{A})\text{.}

•

\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}
annihilates the vertical metric:

(2.23)

\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}\mathsf{g}=0\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}\mathsf{g}^{-1}=0\text{.}

Proof.

See
[
45
, Definition 2.22, Proposition 2.23]
.
∎

In summary, Proposition
2.22
states that
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
extends the vertical Levi-Civita connections
\mathsf{D}
to all directions along
\mathscr{M}
, satisfy the same algebraic properties as the usual Levi-Civita derivatives (such as
\nabla
and
\mathsf{D}
), and are compatible with the vertical metric
\mathsf{g}
.

Remark 2.23
.

If we identify vertical tensor fields with spacetime tensor fields via Remark
2.3
, then
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
can alternately be defined as the Levi-Civita connection associated with
\rho^{2}g
.

Next, we construct mixed tensor bundles and their associated connections.

Definition 2.24
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment.
We then define the
mixed bundle
of rank
(\kappa,\lambda;k,l)
over
\mathscr{M}
to be the tensor product bundle given by

(2.24)

T^{\kappa}_{\lambda}\mathsf{V}^{k}_{l}\mathscr{M}:=T^{\kappa}_{\lambda}\mathscr{M}\otimes\mathsf{V}^{k}_{l}\mathscr{M}\text{.}

We refer to sections of
T^{\kappa}_{\lambda}\mathsf{V}^{k}_{l}\mathscr{M}
as
mixed tensor fields
of rank
(\kappa,\lambda;k,l)
.

Moreover, we define the connection
\bar{\nabla}
on
T^{\kappa}_{\lambda}\mathsf{V}^{k}_{l}\mathscr{M}
to be the tensor product connection of the spacetime connection
\nabla
on
T^{\kappa}_{\lambda}\mathscr{M}
and the vertical connection
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
on
\mathsf{V}^{k}_{l}\mathscr{M}
.
More specifically, given any vector field
X
on
\mathscr{M}
, tensor field
G
on
\mathscr{M}
, and vertical tensor field
\mathsf{B}
, we have

\bar{\nabla}_{X}(G\otimes\mathsf{B}):=\nabla_{X}G\otimes\mathsf{B}+G\otimes\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{X}\mathsf{B}\text{.}

Less formally, mixed tensor fields are those with some components designated as “spacetime” and other designated as “vertical”.
The mixed connections are then defined on mixed tensor fields by acting like
\nabla
on the spacetime components and like
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
on the vertical components.

Remark 2.25
.

Any tensor field of rank
(\kappa,\lambda)
on
\mathscr{M}
can be viewed as a mixed tensor field, with rank
(\kappa,\lambda;0,0)
.
Similarly, any vertical tensor field is a mixed tensor field.

Proposition 2.26
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment.
Then, given any vector field
X
on
\mathscr{M}
:

•

The following holds for any mixed tensor fields
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{B}
:
38
38

38

As usual,
\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}
is defined componentwise by multiplying the components of
\mathbf{A}
and
\mathbf{B}
.

(2.25)

\bar{\nabla}_{X}(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B})=\bar{\nabla}_{X}\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}+\mathbf{A}\otimes\bar{\nabla}_{X}\mathbf{B}\text{.}

•

The operator
\bar{\nabla}_{X}
annihilates both the spacetime and the vertical metrics:

(2.26)

\bar{\nabla}_{X}g=0\text{,}\qquad\bar{\nabla}_{X}g^{-1}=0\text{,}\qquad\bar{\nabla}_{X}\mathsf{g}=0\text{,}\qquad\bar{\nabla}_{X}\mathsf{g}^{-1}=0\text{.}

Proof.

See
[
45
, Proposition 2.28]
.
∎

In summary, the mixed connections
\bar{\nabla}
naturally extend
\nabla
and
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
to mixed fields, have the same algebraic properties as the usual Levi-Civita derivatives, and are compatible with both
g
and
\mathsf{g}
.

The main reason for expanding our scope from vertical to mixed tensor fields is that we can now make sense of higher covariant derivatives of mixed tensor fields:

Definition 2.27
.

Given an FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{M},g)
and a mixed tensor field
\mathbf{A}
of rank
(\kappa,\lambda;k,l)
:

•

The
mixed covariant differential
of
\mathbf{A}
is the mixed tensor field
\bar{\nabla}\mathbf{A}
, of rank
(\kappa,\lambda+1;k,l)
, that maps each vector field
X
on
\mathscr{M}
(in the extra covariant slot) to
\bar{\nabla}_{X}\mathbf{A}
.

•

The
mixed Hessian

\bar{\nabla}^{2}\mathbf{A}
is then defined to be the mixed covariant differential of
\bar{\nabla}\mathbf{A}
.

•

In particular, we now define
\bar{\Box}\mathbf{A}
—the
wave operator
applied to
\mathbf{A}
—to be the
g
-trace of
\bar{\nabla}^{2}\mathbf{A}
, where the trace is applied to the two derivative components.

Remark 2.28
.

In this article, we will only consider
\bar{\Box}
applied to vertical tensor fields.
The main novelty, and subtlety, in this case is that the outer derivative acts as a spacetime derivative
\nabla
on the inner derivative slot and as a vertical derivative
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
on the vertical tensor field itself.

Finally, we list the following identities, which will be useful in upcoming computations:

Proposition 2.29
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment.
In addition, let
(U,\varphi)
denote coordinates on
\mathscr{I}
, and let
\Gamma
and
\mathsf{\Gamma}
denote Christoffel symbols in
\varphi_{\rho}
-coordinates for
\nabla
and
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
, respectively:

(2.27)

\nabla_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}:=\Gamma^{\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}\partial_{\gamma}\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\alpha}\partial_{b}:=\mathsf{\Gamma}^{c}_{\alpha b}\partial_{c}\text{.}

Then, the following relations hold:

(2.28)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\rho\rho}^{\alpha}=-\rho^{-1}\delta^{\alpha}{}_{\rho}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\Gamma_{a\rho}^{\rho}=0\text{,}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{a\rho}^{c}=-\rho^{-1}\delta^{c}{}_{a}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ad}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\Gamma_{\rho a}^{c}-\mathsf{\Gamma}_{\rho a}^{c}=-\rho^{-1}\delta^{c}{}_{a}\text{,}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{ab}^{\rho}=\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ab}-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ab}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\Gamma_{ab}^{c}-\mathsf{\Gamma}_{ab}^{c}=0\text{.}

Furthermore, for any mixed tensor field
\mathbf{A}
of rank
(\kappa,\lambda;k,l)
, we have, in
\varphi
- and
\varphi_{\rho}
-coordinates,

(2.29)

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\gamma}\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\gamma}(\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}})+\sum_{i=1}^{\kappa}\Gamma^{\alpha_{i}}_{\gamma\delta}\,\mathbf{A}^{\hat{\alpha}_{i}\![\delta]}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\sum_{j=1}^{\lambda}\Gamma^{\delta}_{\gamma\beta_{j}}\,\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\hat{\beta}_{j}\![\delta]}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{a_{i}}_{\gamma d}\,\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![d]}{}_{\bar{b}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{d}_{\gamma b_{j}}\,\mathbf{A}^{\bar{\alpha}}{}_{\bar{\beta}}{}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![d]}\text{,}

where
\bar{\alpha}:=\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{\kappa}
,
\bar{\beta}:=\beta_{1}\dots\beta_{\lambda}
,
\bar{a}:=a_{1}\dots a_{k}
, and
\bar{b}:=b_{1}\dots b_{l}
.

Proof.

(
2.28
) follows from (
2.7
) and (
2.20
), while (
2.29
) follows from Definition
2.24
.
∎

2.4.
Some General Formulas

Next, we provide some general formulas for vertical tensor fields, as well as relations between spacetime and vertical tensor fields.
These will be used for deriving many of the equations we will need for proving Theorem
1.5
.
Moreover, we give a general development here, as these will be of independent interest beyond the present article.

Remark 2.30
.

Similar formulas were proved in
[
44
]
, however here we need more details for the “error” terms.
Thus, we provide full derivations of these properties in Appendix
7
.

First, we devise some schematic notations, originally from
[
50
]
, for describing error terms:

Definition 2.31
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment.
Given any
N\geq 1
and vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{A}_{N}
on
\mathscr{M}
, we write
\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{A}_{1},\dots,\mathsf{A}_{N})
to represent any vertical tensor field of the form

(2.30)

\sum_{j=1}^{J}\mathcal{Q}_{j}(\mathsf{A}_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathsf{A}_{N})\text{,}\qquad J\geq 0\text{,}

where each
\mathcal{Q}_{j}
,
1\leq j\leq J
, is a composition of zero or more of the following operations:

•

Component permutations.

•

(Non-metric) contractions.

•

Multiplications by a scalar constant.

Definition 2.32
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment.

•

For any
N\geq 1
, we define the shorthands

(2.31)

\mathsf{g}^{N}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\mathsf{g}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{g}^{-N}:=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\mathsf{g}^{-1}\text{.}

•

For brevity, we also use the shorthand
\mathsf{m}
to denote the
\rho
-derivative of
\mathsf{g}
:

(2.32)

\mathsf{m}:=\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}\text{.}

Next, we establish some general identities for vertical tensor fields:

Proposition 2.33
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment.
Then:

•

The following commutation identities hold for any vertical tensor field
\mathsf{A}
:
39
39

39

To clarify, if
\mathsf{A}
has rank
(k,l)
, then
\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A})
denotes
\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}
acting on the rank
(k,l+1)
field
\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A}
, while
\mathsf{D}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})
denotes the covariant differential of the rank
(k,l)
field
\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}
.
A similar point holds for
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A})
and
\mathsf{D}(\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})
.

(2.33)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}(\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})\text{.}

•

The following identity holds for vertical tensor field
\mathsf{A}
and
p\in\mathbb{R}
,

(2.34)

\bar{\Box}(\rho^{p}\mathsf{A})=\rho^{p}\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}+2p\rho^{p+1}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}-p(n-p)\rho^{p}\mathsf{A}+p\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\rho^{p}\mathsf{A})\text{.}

•

Furthermore, the following hold for any vertical tensor field
\mathsf{A}
:

(2.35)

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{2}\mathsf{A}-(n-1)\rho\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}+\rho^{2}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\mathsf{D}_{ab}\mathsf{A}+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.1
.
∎

Lastly, the key differential equations behind the proof of Theorem
1.5
are given in terms of spacetime tensor fields.
On the other hand, our main quantities of analysis are vector tensor fields, for which one can make sense of limits at the conformal boundary.
Thus, we will need to convert our equations for spacetime quantities into corresponding equations for vertical quantities.

Proposition 2.34
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment.
Let
F
be a rank
(0,l_{1}+l_{2})
tensor field on
\mathscr{M}
, and let
\mathsf{f}
be the rank
(0,l_{2})
vertical tensor field defined, in any coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
, by

(2.36)

\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}:=F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}\text{,}

where the multi-index
\bar{\rho}:=\rho\dots\rho
represents
l_{1}
copies of
\rho
, while
\bar{a}:=a_{1}\dots a_{l_{2}}
.

Then, the following identities hold with respect any coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
,

(2.37)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l_{1}-l_{2}}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l_{1}+l_{2}}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{c\bar{a}}-\rho^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{ca_{j}}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{c\bar{a}}+\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m};\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{c\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\Box F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l_{1}-l_{2}}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{l_{1}+l_{2}}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}+2\rho\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{c\bar{a}}-2\rho\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{a_{j}}(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-(nl_{1}+l_{2})\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad-2\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{a_{j}\hat{a}_{j}}+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{bc\bar{a}}+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{i}a_{j}}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{\bar{a}}+\rho^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{\bar{a}}+\rho^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}+\rho^{2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-3},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}+\rho^{2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}+\rho\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}\text{.}

where
\bar{\rho}
and
\bar{a}
are as defined above, and where:

•

For any
1\leq i\leq l_{1}
, the rank
(0,l_{2}+1)
vertical tensor field
\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i}
is given by

(2.38)

(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{b\bar{a}}:=F_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![b]\bar{a}}\text{.}

•

For any
1\leq j\leq l_{2}
, the rank
(0,l_{2}-1)
vertical tensor field
\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j}
is given by

(2.39)

(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}:=F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\rho]}\text{.}

•

For any
1\leq i,j\leq l_{1}
with
i\neq j
, the rank
(0,l_{2}+2)
vertical field
\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j}
is given by

(2.40)

(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}:=F_{\hat{\rho}_{i,j}\![c,b]\bar{a}}\text{.}

•

For any
1\leq i,j\leq l_{2}
with
i\neq j
, the rank
(0,l_{2}-2)
vertical field
\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j}
is given by

(2.41)

(\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}:=F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{i,j}\![\rho,\rho]}\text{.}

•

For any
1\leq i\leq l_{1}
and
1\leq j\leq l_{2}
, the rank
(0,l_{2})
vertical field
\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j}
is given by

(2.42)

(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{b\hat{a}_{j}}:=F_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![b]\hat{a}_{j}\![\rho]}\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.2
.
∎

3.
The Wave-Transport System

In this section, we establish various geometric identities relating the metrics and curvatures of vacuum FG-aAdS segments.
We then apply these identities in order to derive a system of wave and transport equations that are satisfied by the
difference
of two vacuum FG-aAdS geometries.
This wave-transport system will be central to the proof of our main results.

3.1.
The Structure Equations

We now consider several identities connecting different geometric quantities in vacuum aAdS spacetimes.
We begin by defining vertical tensor fields that capture the nontrivial components of the spacetime Weyl curvature:

Definition 3.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment.
We then define vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{w}^{0}
,
\mathsf{w}^{1}
, and
\mathsf{w}^{2}
—of ranks
(0,4)
,
(0,3)
, and
(0,2)
, respectively—by the formulas

(3.1)

\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}:=\rho^{2}\,W_{abcd}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}:=\rho^{2}\,W_{\rho abc}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}:=\rho^{2}\,W_{\rho a\rho b}\text{.}

In addition, when
n>2
, we let
\mathsf{w}^{\star}
denote the rank
(0,4)
vertical tensor field defined as

(3.2)

\mathsf{w}^{\star}_{abcd}:=\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}-\frac{1}{n-2}(\mathsf{g}_{ad}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bc}+\mathsf{g}_{bc}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ad}-\mathsf{g}_{ac}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bd}-\mathsf{g}_{bd}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac})\text{.}

In both (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), the indices are respect to arbitrary coordinates
(U,\varphi)
(and
\varphi_{\rho}
) on
\mathscr{I}
.

Remark 3.2
.

The reason for the renormalization (
3.2
) is that
\mathsf{w}^{\star}
satisfies a tensorial wave equation (see (
3.6
)) that can be treated with our Carleman estimates, whereas
\mathsf{w}^{0}
does not.

The following three identities, derived in
[
50
]
, relate the spacetime Weyl curvature (expressed in terms of (
3.1
)) to the vertical metric and its derivatives.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment.
Then, the following identities hold with respect to an arbitrary coordinate system
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
:

(3.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{1}_{cab}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{m}_{ac}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{D}_{a}\mathsf{m}_{bc}\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{R}_{abcd}+\frac{1}{4}(\mathsf{m}_{ad}\mathsf{m}_{bc}-\mathsf{m}_{bd}\mathsf{m}_{ac})+\frac{1}{2}\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{g}_{bd}\mathsf{m}_{ac}+\mathsf{g}_{ac}\mathsf{m}_{bd}-\mathsf{g}_{ad}\mathsf{m}_{bc}-\mathsf{g}_{bc}\mathsf{m}_{ad})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{m}_{ab}+\frac{1}{2}\rho^{-1}\mathsf{m}_{ab}+\frac{1}{4}\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathsf{m}_{ad}\mathsf{m}_{bc}\text{.}

Proof.

See
[
50
, Proposition 2.25]
and (
2.32
).
∎

Next, we derive identities satisfied by the spacetime Weyl curvature itself.
First, we recall the more familiar formulas in terms of spacetime tensor fields:

Proposition 3.4
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment.
Then, the following identities hold for the spacetime Weyl curvature
W
, with respect to any coordinates on
\mathscr{M}
:

(3.4)

\displaystyle g^{\mu\nu}\nabla_{\mu}W_{\nu\beta\gamma\delta}

\displaystyle=0\text{,}

\displaystyle(\Box+2n)W_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}

\displaystyle=g^{\lambda\kappa}g^{\mu\nu}(2W_{\lambda\alpha\mu\delta}W_{\kappa\beta\nu\gamma}-2W_{\lambda\alpha\mu\gamma}W_{\kappa\beta\nu\delta}-W_{\lambda\mu\gamma\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\kappa\nu})\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.3
.
∎

The next step is to reformulate Proposition
3.4
in terms of the corresponding vertical quantities
\mathsf{w}^{0}
,
\mathsf{w}^{1}
,
\mathsf{w}^{2}
.
We begin with the
vertical Bianchi identities
for
\mathsf{w}^{0}
,
\mathsf{w}^{1}
, and
\mathsf{w}^{2}
:

Proposition 3.5
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment.
Then, the following vertical Bianchi identities hold with respect to any coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
:

(3.5)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bad}+(n-2)\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})_{ab}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})_{ab}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{eabc}+(n-2)\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})_{abc}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac}-\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})_{abc}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{a}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bcd}-\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{acd}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ad}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bc}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{bc}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ad}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ac}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bd}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{bd}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})_{abcd}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})_{abcd}\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.4
.
∎

In the following, we derive the wave equations satisfied by
\mathsf{w}^{\star}
,
\mathsf{w}^{1}
, and
\mathsf{w}^{2}
:

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment, and let
n>2
.
Then,
40
40

40

The assumption
n>2
is needed only to make sense of
\mathsf{w}^{\star}
; the first two parts of (
3.6
) also hold when
n=2
.

(3.6)

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{w}^{2}+2(n-2)\mathsf{w}^{2}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-3},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{w}^{2},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{0},\mathsf{w}^{2})\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{w}^{1}+(n-1)\mathsf{w}^{1}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{0})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{0},\mathsf{w}^{1})\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{w}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-3},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{2},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{w}^{2},\mathsf{w}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{w}^{2},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{0},\mathsf{w}^{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{0},\mathsf{w}^{2})\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.5
.
∎

Finally, we list some asymptotic bounds for various geometric quantities.
To make these easier to state, we construct the following notations, which were also used in
[
44
]
.

Definition 3.7
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment, fix an integer
M\geq 0
, and let
h\in C^{\infty}(\mathscr{M})
.

•

We use the notation
\mathscr{O}_{M}(h)
to refer to any vertical tensor field
\mathsf{a}
satisfying the following bound for any compact coordinate system
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
:

(3.7)

|\mathsf{a}|_{M,\varphi}\lesssim_{\varphi}h\text{.}

•

Given a vertical tensor field
\mathsf{B}
, we use the notation
\mathscr{O}_{M}(h;\mathsf{B})
to refer to any vertical tensor field of the form
\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{e},\mathsf{B})
, where
\mathsf{e}
is a vertical tensor field satisfying
\mathsf{e}=\mathscr{O}_{M}(h)
.

Proposition 3.8
.

Suppose
(\mathscr{M},g)
is a vacuum FG-aAdS segment, and assume
(\mathscr{M},g)
is regular to order
M\geq n+2
.
41
41

41

See Definition
2.14
.

Then, the following properties hold for
\mathsf{g}
and
\mathsf{m}
:

(3.8)

\mathsf{g}=\mathscr{O}_{M}(1)\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{g}^{-1}=\mathscr{O}_{M}(1)\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{m}=\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1)=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho)\text{,}\qquad\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{m}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)\text{.}

Furthermore, we have the following properties for
\mathsf{w}^{0}
,
\mathsf{w}^{1}
, and
\mathsf{w}^{2}
:

(3.9)

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{0}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1)\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{1}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho)\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1)\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{2}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho)\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1)\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{\star}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{\star}=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1)\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.6
.
∎

3.2.
Difference Relations

We now consider two aAdS geometries on a common manifold, and we derive equations relating quantities representing the
difference
between two geometries.
More specifically, we consider in this subsection
two
vacuum aAdS metrics on a common manifold—that is, we consider two vacuum FG-aAdS segments
(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
, with

(3.10)

\mathscr{M}:=(0,\rho_{0}]\times\mathscr{I}\text{,}\qquad g:=\rho^{-2}(d\rho^{2}+\mathsf{g})\text{,}\qquad\check{g}:=\rho^{-2}(d\rho^{2}+\check{\mathsf{g}})\text{.}

Note that
g
and
\check{g}
live on a common manifold
\mathscr{M}
, and they share a common “radial” variable
\rho
that is used for the Fefferman-Graham expansion with respect to both
g
and
\check{g}
.

To simplify matters, we will adopt the conventions for describing two geometries:

Definition 3.9
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
denote two vacuum FG-aAdS segments, as in (
3.10
).

•

We use the notations described in Section
2
for both
(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
.

•

In particular, all objects associated with
\check{g}
will use the usual notation, but with a “check” added above the symbol (e.g.
\mathsf{g}
and
\check{\mathsf{g}}
for the associated vertical metrics).

Remark 3.10
.

Since
(\mathscr{M},g)
,
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
in Definition
3.9
have the same vertical bundles, the calculus for vertical tensors developed in Section
2
also applies to differences of corresponding geometric quantities, e.g.
\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}
and
\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}
.
In particular, all the equations in Propositions
3.3
,
3.5
,
3.6
hold for both the
g
- and
\check{g}
-geometries, hence we can consider differences of all these identities.

We now derive a closed system of wave and transport equations for the difference between two aAdS geometries.
Later, we will show in Section
5
that the proof of Theorem
1.5
reduces precisely to unique continuation results, and hence Carleman estimates, for this coupled system.

To obtain this system, it will be convenient to have some additional auxiliary quantities:

Definition 3.11
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
denote two vacuum FG-aAdS segments.

•

We define the rank
(0,2)
vertical tensor field
\mathsf{Q}
to be the solution of the transport equation

(3.11)

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{Q}_{ca}=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}[\mathsf{m}_{ad}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{ce}-\mathsf{m}_{cd}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{ae}]\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{Q}\rightarrow^{0}0\text{.}

•

We define the rank
(0,3)
vertical tensor field
\mathsf{B}
by

(3.12)

\mathsf{B}_{cab}:=\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ab}-\mathsf{D}_{a}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cb}-\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{Q}_{ca}

•

We define the vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{W}^{2},\mathsf{W}^{1},\mathsf{W}^{\star}
—of ranks
(0,2)
,
(0,3)
,
(0,4)
, respectively—as follows: given a multi-index
\bar{a}
of appropriate length, we set

(3.13)

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{2}_{\bar{a}}:=(\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2})_{\bar{a}}+\mathsf{g}^{bc}\sum_{j=1}^{2}\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}_{\bar{a}_{j}[b]}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{a_{j}c}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\bar{a}:=(a_{1}a_{2})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{1}_{\bar{a}}:=(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1})_{\bar{a}}+\mathsf{g}^{bc}\sum_{j=1}^{3}\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1}_{\bar{a}_{j}[b]}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{a_{j}c}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\bar{a}:=(a_{1}a_{2}a_{3})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{\star}_{\bar{a}}:=(\mathsf{w}^{\star}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{\star})_{\bar{a}}+\mathsf{g}^{bc}\sum_{j=1}^{4}\check{\mathsf{w}}^{\star}_{\bar{a}_{j}[b]}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{a_{j}c}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\bar{a}:=(a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}a_{4})\text{.}

In the above, all indices are with respect to any arbitrary coordinate system
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
.

Proposition 3.12
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
denote two vacuum FG-aAdS segments.
Then:

•

The following asymptotic relations hold (with
\mathsf{Q}
,
\mathsf{B}
be as in Definition
3.11
):

(3.14)

\mathsf{g}^{-1}-\check{\mathsf{g}}^{-1}=\mathscr{O}_{M}(1;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{Q}=\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(\rho)=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{2})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{B}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)\text{.}

•

The following hold with respect to any coordinate system
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
, where the Christoffel symbols
\Gamma
,
\check{\Gamma}
,
\mathsf{\Gamma}
,
\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}}
are defined as in Proposition
2.29
:

(3.15)

\displaystyle(\Gamma-\check{\Gamma})^{\alpha}_{\rho\rho}=(\Gamma-\check{\Gamma})^{\rho}_{\alpha\rho}

\displaystyle=0\text{,}

\displaystyle(\Gamma-\check{\Gamma})^{\rho}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ab}+\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{ab}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\Gamma-\check{\Gamma})^{a}_{\rho b}=(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{a}_{\rho b}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})^{a}{}_{b}+\mathscr{O}_{M}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})^{a}{}_{b}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\Gamma-\check{\Gamma})^{c}_{ab}=(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{c}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{cd}[\mathsf{D}_{a}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{db}+\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{da}-\mathsf{D}_{d}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ab}]

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M}(1;\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}))^{c}_{ab}\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.7
.
∎

The following two propositions contain our main wave-transport system.
In particular, the key step in the proof of our main result is a unique continuation result on this system.

Proposition 3.13
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
denote two vacuum FG-aAdS segments.
Then,

(3.16)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{Q}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}[\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})]

\displaystyle=-2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{W}^{2}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1;\mathsf{Q})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{B}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{W}^{1})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})+\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{B})\text{.}

In addition, the following derivative transport equations hold:

(3.17)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}\mathsf{Q}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}))+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{D}\mathsf{Q})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}[\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})]

\displaystyle=-2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{D}\mathsf{W}^{2}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1;\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}))+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1;\mathsf{D}\mathsf{Q})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}))

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-4}(1;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-4}(1;\mathsf{Q})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}\mathsf{B}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{D}\mathsf{W}^{1})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}))+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{D}\mathsf{Q})+\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1;\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}))

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{D}\mathsf{B})+\mathscr{O}_{M-4}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-4}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{B})\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.8
.
∎

Proposition 3.14
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
denote two vacuum FG-aAdS segments.
Then,

(3.18)

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{W}^{2}+2(n-2)\mathsf{W}^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{F}^{2}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{W}^{1}+(n-1)\mathsf{W}^{1}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{F}^{1}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{W}^{\star}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{F}^{\star}\text{,}

where each
\mathsf{F}\in\{\mathsf{F}^{2},\mathsf{F}^{1},\mathsf{F}^{\star}\}
is schematically of the form

(3.19)

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M-4}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{\bar{a}}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{Q})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}))_{\bar{a}}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{D}\mathsf{Q})_{\bar{a}}+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}))_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{D}\mathsf{B})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\{\mathsf{W}^{\star},\mathsf{W}^{1},\mathsf{W}^{2}\}}[\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{V})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{3};\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V})]_{\bar{a}}\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.9
.
∎

Finally, we collect here convenient forms for the differences of the Bianchi equations (
3.5
).
These will be needed in another step in the proof of our main result—showing that the quantities relating to the differences of two geometries vanish to arbitrarily high order.

Proposition 3.15
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
denote two vacuum FG-aAdS segments.
Then,

(3.20)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}[\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})]

\displaystyle=-2\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}[\rho^{2-n}(\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2})]

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M}(\rho^{2-n};\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1}))+\mathscr{O}_{M-4}(\rho^{3-n};\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{2-n};\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho^{3-n};\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}))

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{3-n};\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{3-n};\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}[\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1})]

\displaystyle=\rho^{-1}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}))+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{-1};\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1;\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}))

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1;\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1}))+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{-1};\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{D}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}))+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0})\text{.}

Proof.

See Appendix
7.10
.
∎

Remark 3.16
.

The system (
3.20
) is sufficient to obtain higher-order vanishing of the differences of geometric quantities at the boundary (see Proposition
5.3
).
However, we will need to work with the larger system (
3.16
)–(
3.18
)—containing renormalized quantities
\mathsf{Q}
,
\mathsf{B}
,
\mathsf{W}^{\star}
,
\mathsf{W}^{1}
,
\mathsf{W}^{2}
—in order to close the Carleman estimates, within which we cannot afford a loss in derivatives.

4.
The Carleman Estimates

In this section, we state the two Carleman estimates for vertical tensor fields that constitute the main analytic ingredients for the proof of our main results.

4.1.
The Wave Carleman Estimate

The first Carleman estimate we discuss is that for wave equations—namely, the main results obtained in
[
18
,
30
,
31
,
45
]
.
We begin by discussing the best-known conditions needed on the conformal boundary for such an estimate to hold.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment, and let
\mathfrak{h}
be a Riemannian metric on
\mathscr{I}
.

•

We can also view
\mathfrak{h}
as a
\rho
-independent vertical Riemannian metric.
42
42

42

See Remark
2.3
.

•

For a vertical tensor field
\mathsf{A}
, we write
|\mathsf{A}|_{\mathfrak{h}}
to denote its pointwise
\mathfrak{h}
-norm.
In other words, if
\mathsf{A}
has rank
(k,l)
, then with respect to any coordinate system
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
, we have

(4.1)

|\mathsf{A}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}=\mathfrak{h}_{a_{1}c_{1}}\dots\mathfrak{h}_{a_{k}c_{k}}\mathfrak{h}^{b_{1}d_{1}}\dots\mathfrak{h}^{b_{l}d_{l}}\mathsf{A}^{a_{1}\dots a_{k}}_{b_{1}\dots b_{l}}\mathsf{A}^{c_{1}\dots c_{k}}_{d_{1}\dots d_{l}}\text{.}

Remark 4.2
.

The metric
\mathfrak{h}
is only used as a coordinate-independent way to measure the sizes of vertical tensor fields.
Our main results will not depend on a particular choice of
\mathfrak{h}
.

Definition 4.3

(Definition 3.1 of
[
18
]
)
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment, let
\mathfrak{h}
be a Riemannian metric on
\mathscr{I}
, and let
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{I}
be open with compact closure.
We say
(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g})
satisfies the
generalized null convexity criterion
(or
GNCC
) iff there exist
\eta\in C^{4}(\bar{\mathscr{D}})
and
c>0
satisfying

(4.2)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\left(\mathfrak{D}^{2}\eta+\frac{1}{n-2}\,\eta\,\mathfrak{Rc}\right)(\mathfrak{Z},\mathfrak{Z})>c\eta\,\mathfrak{h}(\mathfrak{Z},\mathfrak{Z})&\text{in }\mathscr{D}\text{,}\\
\eta>0&\text{in }\mathscr{D}\text{,}\\
\eta=0&\text{on }\partial\mathscr{D}\text{,}\end{cases}

for all vectors fields
\mathfrak{Z}
on
\mathscr{D}
satisfying
\mathfrak{g}(\mathfrak{Z},\mathfrak{Z})=0
.

Remark 4.4
.

In Definition
4.3
, we specialized to vacuum FG-aAdS segments.
However, the GNCC can be directly extended, as in
[
18
]
, to strongly FG-aAdS segments that are not necessarily vacuum.
In the more general setting, the Ricci curvature
\mathfrak{Rc}
in (
4.2
) is replaced by
-\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
.
43
43

43

Note that for vacuum FG-aAdS segments, (
2.14
) implies
-\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}(\mathfrak{Z},\mathfrak{Z})=\frac{1}{n-2}\,\mathfrak{Rc}(\mathfrak{Z},\mathfrak{Z})
for any
\mathfrak{g}
-null vector field
\mathfrak{Z}
.

Next, we recall some quantities that will be essential to our Carleman estimates:

Definition 4.5
.

Assume the setting of Definition
4.3
—in particular, let
(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g})
satisfy the GNCC, with
\eta
satisfying (
4.2
).
Within this setting, we define the following additional quantities:

•

Let
f_{\eta}:=f\in C^{4}((0,\rho_{0}]\times\mathscr{D})
denote the function

(4.3)

f:=\rho\eta^{-1}\text{.}

•

In addition, given
f_{\star}>0
, we define the domain

(4.4)

\Omega_{f_{\star}}:=\{z\in\mathscr{M}\mid f(z)<f_{\star}\}\text{.}

We can now state the precise form of the Carleman estimate for wave equations from
[
18
]
.
Here, to slightly simplify the presentation, we express this in a less general form than in
[
18
]
.

Theorem 4.6

(Theorem 5.11 of
[
18
]
)
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment.
In addition:

•

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a Riemannian metric on
\mathscr{I}
, and
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{I}
be open with compact closure.

•

Assume
(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g})
satisfies the GNCC, with
\eta\in C^{4}(\bar{\mathscr{D}})
as in (
4.2
) and
\mathfrak{h}
as above.

•

Fix integers
k,l\geq 0
and a constant
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
.

Then, there exist
\mathcal{C}_{0}\geq 0
and
\mathcal{C},\mathcal{C}_{b}>0
(depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
\mathfrak{h}
,
\mathscr{D}
,
k
,
l
) such that for any
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
with

(4.5)

2\kappa\geq n-1+\mathcal{C}_{0}\text{,}\qquad\kappa^{2}-(n-2)\kappa+\sigma-(n-1)-\mathcal{C}_{0}\geq 0\text{,}

and for any constants
f_{\star},\lambda,p>0
with

(4.6)

0<f_{\star}\ll_{\mathsf{g},\mathfrak{h},\mathscr{D},k,l}1\text{,}\qquad\lambda\gg_{\mathsf{g},\mathfrak{h},\mathscr{D},k,l}|\kappa|+|\sigma|\text{,}\qquad 0<2p<1\text{,}

the following Carleman estimate holds for any vertical tensor field
\mathsf{\Phi}
on
\mathscr{M}
of rank
(k,l)
such that both
\mathsf{\Phi}
and
\smash{\bar{\nabla}}\mathsf{\Phi}
vanish identically on
\{f=f_{\star}\}
:
44
44

44

For notational convenience, we replaced the parameter
\lambda
in
[
18
]
by
\lambda/2
here.

(4.7)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star}}}e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}|(\bar{\Box}+\sigma)\mathsf{\Phi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\mathcal{C}_{b}\lambda^{3}\limsup_{\rho_{\star}\searrow 0}\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star}}\cap\{\rho=\rho_{\star}\}}[|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\partial_{\rho}}(\rho^{-\kappa}\mathsf{\Phi})|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+|\mathsf{D}(\rho^{-\kappa}\mathsf{\Phi})|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+|\rho^{-\kappa-1}\mathsf{\Phi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}]\,d\mu_{\mathsf{g}}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathcal{C}\lambda\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star}}}e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}(\rho^{4}|\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\partial_{\rho}}\mathsf{\Phi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{\Phi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+f^{2p}|\mathsf{\Phi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\,d\mu_{g}\text{.}

Here,
d\mu_{g}
denotes the volume form on
\mathscr{M}
induced by the spacetime metric
g
, while
d\mu_{\mathsf{g}}
denotes the volume forms on the level sets of
\rho
induced by the vertical metric
\mathsf{g}
.

Remark 4.7
.

We note that Theorem
4.6
only considers vacuum FG-aAdS segments, whereas the more general
[
18
, Theorem 5.11]
also allows for some non-vacuum FG-aAdS segments (under a more general GNCC).
Moreover,
[
18
, Theorem 5.11]
allows for an additional first-order term in the wave equation that is vanishing at a slower ‘‘critical” rate toward the conformal boundary.
45
45

45

See the quantity
X
in
[
18
, Theorem 5.11]
; however, we will not need this extra generality here.

Remark 4.8
.

If
k=l=0
(i.e.
\mathsf{\Phi}
is scalar), then Theorem
4.6
holds with
\mathcal{C}_{0}=0
; see
[
18
]
.

4.2.
The Transport Carleman Estimate

Next, we prove a simple Carleman estimate for transport equations, in the same setting and with the same weights as in Theorem
4.6
.

Proposition 4.9
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment.
In addition:

•

Let
\mathfrak{h}
be a Riemannian metric on
\mathscr{I}
, and
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{I}
be open with compact closure.

•

Assume
(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g})
satisfies the GNCC, with
\eta\in C^{4}(\bar{\mathscr{D}})
as in (
4.2
) and
\mathfrak{h}
as above.

Then, for any
s\geq 0
,
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\lambda,f_{\star},p>0
satisfying

(4.8)

2\kappa\geq\max(n-2,s-3)\text{,}\qquad 0<2p<1\text{,}\qquad 0<f_{\ast}\ll_{\mathsf{g},\mathscr{D}}1\text{,}

there exist
\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\prime}>0
(depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
\mathfrak{h}
,
\mathscr{D}
) such that for every vertical tensor field
\Psi
on
\mathscr{M}
,

(4.9)

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star}}}e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\rho^{s+2}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{g}+\mathcal{C}_{b}^{\prime}\lambda\limsup_{\rho_{\star}\searrow 0}\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star}}\cap\{\rho=\rho_{\ast}\}}\rho^{s}|\rho^{-\kappa-1}\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{\mathsf{g}}

\displaystyle\quad\geq\mathcal{C}\lambda\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star}}}e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{g}\text{.}

Proof.

Using that both
\mathfrak{h}
and
\eta
are
\rho
-independent, and recalling (
4.3
), we obtain

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\left(e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\right)+(2\kappa+2-s+\lambda f^{p})e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n-1}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}\text{.}

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to the right-hand side of the above and rearranging yields

(4.11)

\displaystyle(2\kappa+2-s)e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n-1}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\left(e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{4}\lambda^{-1}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n+1}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\text{.}

We now integrate the above inequality over the region
\Omega_{f_{\star},\rho_{\star}}:=\Omega_{f_{\star}}\cap\{\rho>\rho_{\star}\}
for an arbitrary
0<\rho_{\star}\ll f_{\star}
, first over level sets of
\rho
with respect to the
\rho
-independent volume forms
d\mu_{\mathfrak{h}}
induced by
\mathfrak{h}
, and then over
\rho
.
This yields the estimate

\displaystyle(2\kappa+2-s)\lambda\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star},\rho_{\star}}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n-1}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\ d\mu_{\mathfrak{h}}d\rho

\displaystyle\quad\leq\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star},\rho_{\star}}}\left[\frac{1}{4}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n+1}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}-\lambda\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\left(e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\right)\right]d\mu_{\mathfrak{h}}d\rho

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star},\rho_{\star}}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n+1}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{\mathfrak{h}}d\rho+\lambda\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star}}\cap\{\rho=\rho_{\star}\}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}d\mu_{\mathfrak{h}}\text{,}

where in the last step, we applied the fundamental theorem of calculus; note in particular that the ensuing boundary term on
\{f=f_{\star}\}
is negative and can hence be neglected.
The above now yields, for some constants
\mathcal{C}^{\prime},\mathcal{C}_{b,0}^{\prime}>0
(depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
\mathfrak{h}
,
\mathscr{D}
),

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\lambda\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star},\rho_{\star}}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\ d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star},\rho_{\star}}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\rho^{s+2}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{C}_{b,0}^{\prime}\lambda\int_{\Omega_{f_{\star}}\cap\{\rho=\rho_{\star}\}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}d\mu_{\mathsf{g}}\text{,}

since
d\mu_{\mathfrak{h}}
and
d\mu_{\mathsf{g}}
are comparable due to the compactness of
\bar{\mathscr{D}}
, and since (
2.7
) implies

d\mu_{g}=\rho^{-n-1}\,d\mu_{\mathsf{g}}d\rho\text{.}

Finally, letting
\rho_{\star}\searrow 0
in (
4.12
) yields the desired (
4.9
), since

e^{-\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}\leq 1\text{,}\qquad f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{s-n}|\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}=\eta^{2\kappa-(n-2)}\,\rho^{s}|\rho^{-\kappa-1}\mathsf{\Psi}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\text{,}

and since the exponent
2\kappa-(n-2)\geq 0
by (
4.8
).
∎

Remark 4.10
.

Note that the GNCC is not required for Proposition
4.9
and its proof.
However, it is convenient to include this in the statement Proposition
4.9
, as the transport Carleman estimate will be applied concurrently with and in the same setting as the wave Carleman estimate.

5.
Unique Continuation

In this section, we turn toward establishing unique continuation for the Einstein-vacuum equations.
In particular, we prove the following theorem, which can be seen as a precise statement of most of Theorem
1.5
---the special case in which the pair of holographic data are equal.
46
46

46

The remainder of Theorem
1.5
, which deals with gauge covariance issues, is treated in Section
6.1
below.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
n>2
, and let
(\mathscr{M},g)
,
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
be vacuum FG-aAdS segments (on a common aAdS region
\mathscr{M}:=(0,\rho_{0}]\times\mathscr{I}
),
47
47

47

This is the same setup as in Section
3.2
; the reader is referred there for further notational details.
with associated holographic data
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
,
(\mathscr{I},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
(respectively).
In addition, let
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{I}
be open with compact closure, and assume:

•

(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
are regular to some large enough order
M_{0}
(depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
\check{\mathsf{g}}
,
\mathscr{D}
).

•

The holographic data coincide on
\mathscr{D}
:

(5.1)

(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})|_{\mathscr{D}}=(\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})|_{\mathscr{D}}\text{.}

•

(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
(or equivalently,
(\mathscr{D},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
) satisfies the GNCC.

Then,
g=\check{g}
on a neighbourhood of
\{0\}\times\mathscr{D}
(viewed as part of the conformal boundary)—more specifically, there exists some sufficiently small
f_{\star}>0
such that
g=\check{g}
on the region
\Omega_{f_{\star}}
.
48
48

48

See Definition
4.5
for the definition of
\Omega_{f_{\star}}
.

Remark 5.2
.

Note that Theorem
5.1
offers a more precise conclusion than Theorem
1.5
—in the special case (
5.1
), the isometry in Theorem
1.5
is simply the identity map.

The proof of Theorem
5.1
is given in the remainder of this section.
Throughout, we assume the hypotheses of Theorem
5.1
hold.
Furthermore, we adopt the notational conventions of Section
3.2
regarding quantities defined with respect to
g
and
\check{g}
.

5.1.
Deducing Higher-Order Vanishing

For convenience, we can assume, without loss of generality, that
M_{0}-n
is an even natural number.
The first step is to derive a sufficiently high order of vanishing for
\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}
,
\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}
,
\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}
,
\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1}
, and
\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0}
.
This will ensure that we can apply the Carleman estimates to (variants of) these quantities as needed later in the proof.

The key is to use the equations from Proposition
3.15
satisfied by these quantities to exchange regularity in the vertical directions for higher orders of vanishing.
More specifically, the following proposition is a quantitative statement of the fact that if the our spacetime is regular to high enough order, then we can achieve a corresponding order of vanishing for the above quantities as
\rho\searrow 0
:

Proposition 5.3
.

Consider the quantities
\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}
,
\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}
,
\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}
,
\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1}
,
\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0}
.
Then:

•

There exist vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}
such that

(5.2)

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}=\rho^{M_{0}-2}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\rightarrow^{2}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}=\rho^{M_{0}-3}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}\rightarrow^{2}0\text{.}

•

There exist vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}
such that

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}=\rho^{M_{0}-4}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}\rightarrow^{2}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1}=\rho^{M_{0}-3}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}\rightarrow^{1}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0}=\rho^{M_{0}-4}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}\rightarrow^{2}0\text{.}

Proof.

First, note that since
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}=\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}=\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
, then Theorem
2.16
implies

(5.4)

\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}=\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(k)}\text{,}\qquad\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}=\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}\text{,}\qquad 0\leq k\leq n\text{,}

where the Fefferman-Graham coefficients
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},\dots,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
and
\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},\dots,\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
are defined as in Theorem
2.16
and Corollary
2.18
—with respect to
g
and
\check{g}
, respectively.
The above and Corollary
2.18
then imply that there are vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}
satisfying

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}=\rho^{n}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}=\rho^{n-1}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n}0\text{.}

Moreover, the equations (
3.3
), along with (
5.5
), yield vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}
with

(5.6)

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}=\rho^{n-2}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1}=\rho^{n-1}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n-1}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0}=\rho^{n-2}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n}0\text{.}

The idea now is to use the Bianchi and transport system (
3.20
) to inductively improve the initial orders of vanishing from (
5.5
) and (
5.6
).
Observe that by (
5.5
) and (
5.6
), the quantities

\rho^{-n+2}(\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2})\text{,}\qquad\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0}\text{,}\qquad\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}

all vanish in the boundary limit
\rho\searrow 0
.
Thus, we can now integrate all the equations in (
3.20
) successively from
\rho=0
, without obtaining any boundary terms as
\rho\searrow 0
.

Integrating the third equation in (
3.20
) and using (
5.5
)–(
5.6
), we deduce the existence of a (new) vertical tensor field
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}
(for brevity, we use the same notation as before) such that

(5.7)

\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}=\rho^{n}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n-2}0\text{.}

Integrating next the fifth part of (
3.20
) and using (
5.5
), (
5.6
), and the improved asymptotics for
\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}
in (
5.7
) yields a vertical tensor field
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}
such that

(5.8)

\mathsf{w}^{0}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{0}=\rho^{n}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{0}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n-2}0\text{.}

The next quantity in the hierarchy is
\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}
; integrating the second identity in (
3.20
) and using (
5.5
) and (
5.7
), we deduce the existence of a vertical tensor field
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}
such that

(5.9)

\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}=\rho^{n+1}\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{m}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n-2}0\text{.}

Then, integrating the first part of (
3.20
) and applying (
5.9
) yields an
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}
with

(5.10)

\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}=\rho^{n+2}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{g}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n-2}0\text{.}

Integrating finally the fourth identity of (
3.20
) and recalling (
5.6
)–(
5.10
) results in an
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}
with

(5.11)

\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1}=\rho^{n+1}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}\rightarrow^{M_{0}-n-3}0\text{.}

At this point, the vanishing of all quantities has been improved by two powers of
\rho
at the cost of two derivatives of regularity, compared to the initial asymptotics (
5.5
)–(
5.6
).
By iterating this process (now inserting the improved asymptotics into the right-hand sides of (
3.20
)), we can repeatedly trade two derivatives of regularity for two powers of
\rho
.
Since
M_{0}-n
is even by assumption, the proof is completed after iterating this process
(M_{0}-n-2)/2
times.
∎

Corollary 5.4
.

Let the renormalized quantities
\mathsf{Q}
,
\mathsf{B}
,
\mathsf{W}^{2}
,
\mathsf{W}^{1}
,
\mathsf{W}^{\star}
be as in Definition
3.11
.
Then, there exist vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{Q}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{B}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{2}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{1}}
,
\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{\star}}
such that

(5.12)

\displaystyle\mathsf{Q}=\rho^{M_{0}}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{Q}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{Q}}\rightarrow^{2}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}=\rho^{M_{0}-2}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{B}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{B}}\rightarrow^{1}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{2}=\rho^{M_{0}-4}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{2}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{2}}\rightarrow^{2}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{1}=\rho^{M_{0}-3}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{1}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{1}}\rightarrow^{1}0\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{\star}=\rho^{M_{0}-4}\,\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{\star}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathsf{r}_{\mathsf{W}^{\star}}\rightarrow^{2}0\text{.}

Proof.

This is an immediate consequence of (
3.2
), Definition
3.11
, and Proposition
5.3
.
∎

5.2.
Applying the Carleman Estimates

Fix any Riemannian metric
\mathfrak{h}
on
\mathscr{I}
, and fix additional constants
p,f_{\star}>0
(whose precise values will be determined later) such that

(5.13)

0<2p<1\text{,}\qquad f_{\star}\ll_{\mathsf{g},\mathscr{D}}1\text{.}

Furthermore, as long as
M_{0}
is sufficiently large (depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
\mathscr{D}
), we can find a sufficiently large
\kappa\in\mathbb{R}
(again, the precise value will be determined later) satisfying

(5.14)

1\ll_{n,\mathsf{g},\mathscr{D}}\kappa\leq M_{0}-5\text{.}

Remark 5.5
.

In particular,
p,f_{\star},\kappa
are chosen so that we can apply the Carleman estimates (
4.7
) and (
4.9
) as needed.
Also, although all the above parameters depend on
\mathfrak{h}
, we can instead view this as a dependence on
\mathscr{D}
, as any choice of a Riemannian metric
\mathfrak{h}
on
\bar{\mathscr{D}}
suffices.

In addition, let
f
be as in (
4.3
), where
\eta
is given from the GNCC assumption.
We also let

(5.15)

\chi:=\bar{\chi}\circ f\text{,}

where
\bar{\chi}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,1]
is a smooth cut-off function satisfying

(5.16)

\bar{\chi}(s)=\begin{cases}1&s\leq f_{i}\text{,}\\
0&s\geq f_{e}\text{,}\end{cases}\qquad 0<f_{i}<f_{e}<f_{\star}\text{,}

and we define the following associated subregions of
\Omega_{f_{\star}}
(see (
4.4
)):

(5.17)

\displaystyle\Omega_{i}:=\{f<f_{i}\}\text{,}\qquad\Omega_{e}:=\{f_{i}\leq f<f_{e}\}\text{.}

Lastly, for convenience, we define the following collections of quantities:

(5.18)

\Xi^{\star}:=\{\mathsf{W}^{\star},\mathsf{W}^{1},\mathsf{W}^{2}\}\text{,}\qquad\Upsilon^{\star}:=\{\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}},\mathsf{Q},\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}},\mathsf{B}\}\text{.}

We now apply Theorem
4.6
to
\chi\mathsf{V}
, for each
\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}
, on the region
\Omega_{f_{\star}}
(since
\chi\mathsf{V}
vanishes near
\{f=f_{\star}\}
).
Restricting the right-hand side of (
4.7
) to
\Omega_{i}
, on which
\chi\equiv 1
, we then obtain

(5.19)

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\Omega_{i}} e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(\rho^{2p}|\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\,d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{i}\cup\Omega_{e}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}|(\bar{\Box}+\sigma_{\mathsf{V}})(\chi\mathsf{V})|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{i}\cup\Omega_{e}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{-p}\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(|\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+|\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\,d\mu_{g}

for any
\lambda\gg_{\mathsf{g},\mathscr{D}}1
, where
(\sigma_{\mathsf{W}^{2}},\sigma_{\mathsf{W}^{1}},\sigma_{\mathsf{W}^{\star}})=(2n-4,n-1,0)
, and where

(5.20)

\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{V}}:=\chi(\bar{\Box}+\sigma_{\mathsf{V}})\mathsf{V}\text{,}\qquad\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{V}}:=(\bar{\Box}+\sigma_{\mathsf{V}})(\chi\mathsf{V})-\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{V}}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}\text{.}

Note also in the last step of (
5.19
), we used that
\rho\lesssim f
by definition.

Observe the boundary limit as
\rho_{\star}\searrow 0
in (
4.7
) vanishes due to Corollary
5.4
and (
5.14
).
Moreover, note that since every term in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{V}}
involves at least one derivative of
\chi
, then it is supported in
\Omega_{e}
.
Expanding
\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{V}}
on
\Omega_{i}
using Proposition
3.14
, then (
5.19
) becomes

(5.21)

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\Omega_{i}} e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(\rho^{2p}|\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\,d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{i}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\left[\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(\rho^{4-p}|\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{6-p}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})+\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}(\rho^{2-p}|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4-p}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\right]d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\int_{\Omega_{e}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{-p}\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(|\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+|\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\,d\mu_{g}\text{.}

Next, we can similarly apply the transport Carleman estimate of Proposition
4.9
to each
\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\ast}
on
\Omega_{i}
.
In particular, applying (
4.9
) with
s=2
(for
\mathsf{U}=\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}
) and
s=0
(otherwise) yields

(5.22)

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\Omega_{i}} e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{i}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-p-2\kappa}\left[\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}\setminus\{\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}\}}\rho^{2}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}|\mathscr{L}_{\rho}[\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})]|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\right]d\mu_{g}\text{,}

where
\lambda
is as before.
(The boundary term as
\rho_{\star}\searrow 0
in (
4.9
) vanishes by Proposition
5.3
, Corollary
5.4
, and (
5.14
).
Also, note the cutoff
\chi
is not needed here, since Proposition
4.9
does not require
\Psi
to vanish near
\{f=f_{\ast}\}
.)
Applying (
3.16
) to the right-hand side of (
5.22
), we obtain that

(5.23)

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\Omega_{i}} e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{g}
