\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{i}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\left[\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}\rho^{2-p}|\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}\rho^{2-p}|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\right]d\mu_{g}\text{.}

We can also obtain analogous bounds for
\mathsf{D}\mathsf{U}
, for each
\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\ast}
.
In particular, we apply Proposition
4.9
to the equations (
3.17
) (with
s=5
for
\mathsf{U}=\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}
and
s=3
otherwise), which yields

(5.24)

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\Omega_{i}} e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}\rho^{3}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}\,d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{i}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\left[\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}\rho^{5-p}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}(\rho^{5-p}|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{5-p}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\right]d\mu_{g}\text{.}

Summing the estimates (
5.21
), (
5.23
), and (
5.24
) yields

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\Omega_{i}} e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\left[\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(\rho^{2p}|\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})+\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}(|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{3}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\right]d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{i}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\left[\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(\rho^{2-p}|\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{5-p}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})+\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}(\rho^{2-p}|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4-p}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\right]d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\qquad+\int_{\Omega_{e}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{-p}\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(|\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+|\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\,d\mu_{g}\text{.}

Taking
\lambda
sufficiently large in the above, the integral over
\Omega_{i}
in the right-hand side can be absorbed into the left-hand side, since the quantities in the right contain higher powers of
\rho
than those in the left (and since
\rho
is bounded on
\Omega_{i}
).
As a result, we have

(5.25)

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\Omega_{i}} e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\left[\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(\rho^{2p}|\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})+\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}(|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{3}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\right]d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{e}}e^{-\frac{\lambda f^{p}}{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa}\rho^{-p}\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(|\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+|\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\,d\mu_{g}\text{.}

Finally, using that the Carleman weight

w_{\lambda}(f):=e^{-2\lambda p^{-1}f^{p}}f^{n-2-2\kappa-p}

satisfies
w_{\lambda}(f)\leq w_{\lambda}(f_{i})
on
\Omega_{e}
and
w_{\lambda}(f)\geq w_{\lambda}(f_{i})
on
\Omega_{i}
, we can eliminate
w_{\lambda}(f)
from (
5.25
):

\displaystyle\lambda\int_{\Omega_{i}} \left[\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(\rho^{2p}|\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{4}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{V}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})+\sum_{\mathsf{U}\in\Upsilon^{\star}}(|\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+\rho^{3}|\mathsf{D}\mathsf{U}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\right]d\mu_{g}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{\Omega_{e}}\rho^{-p}\sum_{\mathsf{V}\in\Xi^{\star}}(|\mathcal{F}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2}+|\mathcal{G}_{\mathsf{V}}|_{\mathfrak{h}}^{2})\,d\mu_{g}\text{.}

Since the right-hand side of the above is finite by our vanishing assumptions, we can divide by
\lambda
and take the limit
\lambda\rightarrow\infty
to deduce in particular that
\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}\equiv 0
in
\Omega_{i}
.

Remark 5.6
.

Note that by taking
f_{i}
to be arbitrarily close to
f_{\star}
, the above argument yields that
\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}
vanishes (along with all the other associated difference quantities) on
\Omega_{f_{\star}}
.

Since
g
and
\check{g}
are assumed to be in the FG gauge (
2.7
), and with the same radial coordinate
\rho
, the above immediately yields
g=\check{g}
on
\Omega_{i}
.
This finishes the proof of Theorem
5.1
.

6.
The Main Results

In this section, we state and prove precise versions of the main results of this paper:

•

Section
6.1
is dedicated to the precise statement and proof of Theorem
1.5
.

•

Section
6.2
is dedicated to the precise statements and proofs of Theorems
1.7
and
1.10
.

6.1.
Gauge Covariance

In Theorem
5.1
, we had assumed the two spacetimes in question had identical boundary data.
Here, we extend Theorem
5.1
—we establish the same conclusions, but we weaken the assumptions so that the spacetimes have
gauge-equivalent
boundary data.
The key idea is to apply a specific change of coordinates (preserving the Fefferman-Graham gauge condition) on one spacetime, so that the boundary data for the two spacetimes become equal.

Before discussing the precise result, let us first give a more detailed description of gauge transformations in our current context of FG-aAdS segments.

Definition 6.1
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be an FG-aAdS segment, and let
\mathscr{V}\subset\mathscr{M}
be an open neighbourhood of the conformal boundary.
49
49

49

More precisely, for any
p\in\mathscr{I}
, there exists
\rho_{p}>0
such that
\mathscr{V}
contains
(0,\rho_{p})\times\{p\}
.

A function
\check{\rho}\in C^{\infty}(\mathscr{V})
is called an
FG radius
for
(\mathscr{V},g)
iff

(6.1)

\check{\rho}>0\text{,}\qquad\check{\rho}^{-2}g^{-1}(d\check{\rho},d\check{\rho})=1\text{.}

Remark 6.2
.

Note that
\rho
itself is an FG radius for
(\mathscr{M},g)
.

Informally, we can view such an FG radius as a change of boundary defining function (from
\rho
to
\check{\rho}
) that preserves the Fefferman-Graham gauge condition (
2.7
).
A more detailed justification, in terms of our language of FG-aAdS segments, arises from the following.

Consider a FG radius
\check{\rho}
for
(\mathscr{V},g)
satisfying (
6.1
).
Given
\sigma>0
and
p\in\mathscr{I}
, we identify the pair
(\sigma,p)
with
\gamma_{p}(\sigma)\in\mathscr{V}
, where
\gamma_{p}
is the integral curve of the
\check{\rho}^{2}g
-gradient of
\check{\rho}
that satisfies
50
50

50

In other words,
\gamma_{p}
emanates from the point
p
on the conformal boundary.

\lim_{\sigma\searrow 0}\gamma_{p}(\sigma)=(0,p)\text{.}

(when all the above quantities exist).
Then, given any
\mathscr{J}\subset\mathscr{I}
with
\bar{\mathscr{J}}\subseteq\mathscr{I}
, the above identifies
(0,\check{\rho}_{0}]\times\mathscr{J}
, for some
\check{\rho}_{0}>0
, with an open submanifold
\smash{\check{\mathscr{M}}}\subseteq\mathscr{M}
.
Moreover, by the second part of (
6.1
), the projection onto the
(0,\check{\rho}_{0}]
-component is simply
\check{\rho}
.
As this gradient is (
g
-)normal to the level sets of
\check{\rho}
, then
g
is given—in terms of
\check{\rho}
and frames transported along this gradient—by

(6.2)

g=\check{\rho}^{-2}(d\check{\rho}^{2}+\mathsf{g}^{\ast})\text{,}

for some (
\check{\rho}
-)vertical tensor field
\mathsf{g}^{\ast}
.
In other words,
\tilde{\rho}
generates an FG-aAdS segment, characterized by (
6.2
), that is isometric to (part of) the original FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{M},g)
defined from
\rho
.

It is well-known in the physics literature (see, e.g.,
[
20
,
35
]
) that these transformations preserving the FG gauge can be characterized in terms of corresponding transformations of the boundary data.
Below, we present this in a more rigorous form, adapted to the setting of this paper.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment, and fix
\mathfrak{a}\in C^{\infty}(\mathscr{I})
.
Then, there is a neighbourhood
\mathscr{V}\subset\mathscr{M}
of the conformal boundary and a unique FG radius
\check{\rho}
on
(\mathscr{V},g)
such that

(6.3)

\frac{\check{\rho}}{\rho}\rightarrow^{0}e^{\mathfrak{a}}\text{.}

Furthermore, let
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
and
(\mathscr{I},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
denote the holographic data associated to the FG-aAdS segments constructed from
(\mathscr{M},g)
with respect to
\rho
and
\check{\rho}
, respectively.
Then, there exists a universal algebraic function
\mathscr{F}
(i.e., independent of
\mathscr{M},g,\check{g},\rho,\check{\rho}
) such that

(6.4)

\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}=e^{2\mathfrak{a}}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}\text{,}\qquad\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}=\mathscr{F}(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{R},\dots,\mathfrak{D}^{n-2}\mathfrak{R},\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{D}\mathfrak{a},\dots,\mathfrak{D}^{n}\mathfrak{a})\text{,}

where
\mathfrak{D}
and
\mathfrak{R}
denotes the Levi-Civita connection and Riemann curvature for
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
, respectively.

Proof sketch of Proposition
6.3
.

To obtain
\check{\rho}
, we adapt an argument inspired by the constructions from
[
25
]
.
First, we consider the following ansatz for our desired FG radius:

(6.5)

\check{\rho}:=e^{\mathsf{a}}\rho\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{a}\in C^{\infty}(\mathscr{M})\text{.}

Then, the defining equation (
6.1
) for
\check{\rho}
expands as

1=\rho^{-2}g^{-1}(d\rho,d\rho)+2\rho\cdot\rho^{-2}g^{-1}(d\rho,d\mathsf{a})+\rho^{2}\cdot\rho^{-2}g^{-1}(d\mathsf{a},d\mathsf{a})\text{.}

Since
g
and
\rho
satisfy (
2.7
), the above becomes

(6.6)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=2\rho^{-2}g^{-1}(d\rho,d\mathsf{a})+\rho\cdot\rho^{-2}g^{-1}(d\mathsf{a},d\mathsf{a})

\displaystyle=(2+\rho\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{a})\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{a}+\rho\,\mathsf{g}^{-1}(\mathsf{D}\mathsf{a},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{a})\text{.}

Observe (
6.6
) yields a fully nonlinear equation for
\mathsf{a}
, which can be uniquely solved via the method of characteristics on a neighbourhood
\mathscr{V}
of the conformal boundary given initial data

\lim_{\sigma\searrow 0}\mathsf{a}|_{\sigma}=\mathfrak{a}\text{.}

Then,
\check{\rho}=e^{\mathsf{a}}\rho
yields the FG radius on
\mathscr{V}
which satisfies (
6.3
).

The relations between
(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
and
(\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
, as well as their derivations, are standard in physics literature—see, for instance,
[
20
,
35
]
.
A detailed derivation of the first relation in (
6.4
), in the context of FG-aAdS segments, can be found in
[
18
, Proposition 3.4]
.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

Using (
6.6
), along with the techniques of
[
50
]
, one can derive boundary limits for
\rho
-derivatives of both
\mathsf{a}
and
\check{\rho}
in Proposition
6.3
.
From these limits, one then obtains partial series expansions for
\mathsf{a}
and
\check{\rho}
in terms of powers of
\rho
, similar to those in Corollary
2.18
.
In particular, these expansions justify the ansatz for FG gauge transforms used in
[
18
, Equation (3.4)]
.

Remark 6.5
.

In addition to (
6.4
), there are similar transformation formulas for the other coefficients
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(4)},\dots,\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(\star)}
of the Fefferman-Graham expansion.
(For example,
-\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(2)}
transforms like the Schouten tensor under conformal rescalings—see
[
18
, Proposition 3.4]
for a detailed derivation.)

In particular, Proposition
6.3
implies that two holographic data
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
,
(\mathscr{I},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
that are related via (
6.4
) are associated to the
same
aAdS spacetime, via two different FG gauges on this spacetime.
This motivates the following definition:

Definition 6.6
.

Let
\mathscr{I}
be an
n
-dimensional manifold, let
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
be two Lorentzian metrics on
\mathscr{I}
, and let
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)}
be two symmetric rank-
(0,2)
tensor fields on
\mathscr{I}
.
We say
(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
and
(\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
are
gauge-equivalent
on
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{I}
iff these quantities satisfy (
6.4
) on
\mathscr{D}
for some
\mathfrak{a}\in C^{\infty}(\mathscr{I})
.

We can now state the precise version of Theorem
1.5
, which combines the results of Theorem
5.1
while also taking into account the above gauge covariance:

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
n>2
, and let
(\mathscr{M},g)
,
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
be vacuum FG-aAdS segments (on a common aAdS region
\mathscr{M}
), with associated holographic data
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
,
(\mathscr{I},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
(respectively).
In addition, let
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{I}
be open with compact closure, and assume:

•

(\mathscr{M},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
are regular to some large enough order
M_{0}
(depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
\check{\mathsf{g}}
,
\mathscr{D}
).

•

(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
and
(\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
are gauge-equivalent on
\mathscr{D}
.

•

(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
(or equivalently,
(\mathscr{D},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
) satisfies the GNCC.

Then,
g
and
\check{g}
are isometric near
\{0\}\times\mathscr{D}
(viewed as part of the conformal boundary).
To be more precise, there exists a sufficiently small
f_{\star}>0
and some
\Psi:\Omega_{f_{\star}}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}
such that
51
51

51

In other words,
\Psi
is an isometry that fixes the conformal boundary.

(6.7)

\Psi_{\ast}\check{g}=g\text{,}\qquad\lim_{\sigma\searrow 0}\Psi(\sigma,p)=(0,p)\text{,}\quad p\in\mathscr{D}\text{.}

Proof.

By gauge equivalence, there exists
\mathfrak{a}\in C^{\infty}(\mathscr{I})
so that (
6.4
) holds on
\mathscr{D}
.
Applying Proposition
6.3
, we obtain an FG radius
\check{\rho}
such (
6.3
) holds.
Using
\check{\rho}
(see the discussion below Definition
6.1
), we can identify
(\mathscr{M},g)
with another FG-aAdS segment
(\mathscr{N},g)
, for which the induced holographic data is
(\mathscr{I},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
.
Moreover, applying another isometry that identifies the FG gauges for
(\mathscr{N},g)
and
(\mathscr{M},\check{g})
,
52
52

52

Here, one maps the integral curves of the gradient of
\tilde{\rho}
in
\mathscr{N}
to the integral curves of the gradient of
\rho
in
\mathscr{M}
.
we then arrive at the setting of Theorem
5.1
—two FG-aAdS segments on a common manifold
\mathscr{M}
, with identical data
(\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
on
\mathscr{D}
.

The result now follows by applying Theorem
5.1
; the desired isometry
\Psi
is then constructed by unwinding all the identifications made in the preceding discussion.
∎

Remark 6.8
.

Recall the GNCC is gauge-invariant, so that in the setting of Theorem
6.7
, we have that the GNCC holds for
(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
if and only if it holds for
(\mathscr{D},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
; see
[
18
, Proposition 3.6]
for a proof of this statement.
Therefore, it suffices to assume the GNCC only for
\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)}
.

6.2.
Extension of Symmetries

One application of Theorem
5.1
is that it immediately implies that holographic symmetries on the conformal boundary must be inherited in the bulk spacetime.
Here, we prove two versions of this, one for discrete and the other for continuous symmetries.

The first result is the precise analogue of Theorem
1.10
from the introduction:

Theorem 6.9
.

Let
n>2
, and let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment, with associated holographic data
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
.
Consider also a smooth, invertible function
\phi:\bar{\mathscr{D}}\rightarrow\mathscr{I}
,
53
53

53

More accurately,
\phi:\mathscr{J}\rightarrow\mathscr{I}
for some open
\mathscr{J}\subseteq\mathscr{I}
that contains
\bar{\mathscr{D}}
.
where
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{I}
is open with compact closure.
In addition, assume that the following hold:

•

(\mathscr{M},g)
is regular to some large enough order
M_{0}
(depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
\mathscr{D}
).

•

(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
and
(\phi_{\ast}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\phi_{\ast}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
are gauge-equivalent on
\mathscr{D}
.

•

(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
satisfies the GNCC.

Then,
\phi
extends to an isometry of
g
near
\{0\}\times\mathscr{D}
(viewed as part of the conformal boundary).
To be more precise, there exists a sufficiently small
f_{\star}>0
and some
\Phi:\Omega_{f_{\star}}\rightarrow\mathscr{M}
such that
54
54

54

In other words,
\Phi
is an isometry that asymptotes to
\phi
at the conformal boundary.

(6.8)

\Phi_{\ast}g=g\text{,}\qquad\lim_{\sigma\searrow 0}\Phi(\sigma,p)=(0,\phi(p))\text{,}\quad p\in\mathscr{D}\text{.}

Furthermore, if
\phi
is a
holographic isometry
on
\mathscr{D}
, i.e.,

(6.9)

(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})|_{\mathscr{D}}=(\phi_{\ast}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\phi_{\ast}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})|_{\mathscr{D}}\text{,}

then the bulk isometry
\Phi
is given explicitly as

(6.10)

\Phi(\sigma,p)=(\sigma,\phi(p))\text{,}\qquad(\sigma,p)\in\Omega_{f_{\ast}}\text{.}

Proof.

First, applying a gauge transformation as in the proof of Theorem
6.7
, we can assume (
6.9
) holds.
Thus, it suffices to show that the map
\Phi
given by (
6.10
) is an isometry.

Observe that (
6.10
) implies the pullback
\Phi_{\ast}g
satisfies

\Phi_{\ast}g=\rho^{-2}(d\rho^{2}+\phi^{\ast}\mathsf{g})\text{,}

where
\phi_{\ast}\mathsf{g}
is defined to the the pullback through
\phi
on each level set of
\rho
:

(\phi_{\ast}\mathsf{g})|_{\sigma}:=\phi_{\ast}(\mathsf{g}|_{\sigma})\text{,}\qquad\sigma\in(0,\rho_{0}]\text{.}

In particular,
\Phi_{\ast}g
defines a vacuum FG-aAdS segment (with the same
\rho
as before), whose associated boundary data is
(\phi^{-1}(\mathscr{I}),\phi_{\ast}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\phi_{\ast}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
.
From (
6.9
), we see that
g
and
\Phi_{\ast}g
have the same data on
\mathscr{D}
, hence Theorem
5.1
yields
g=\Phi_{\ast}g
on some
\Omega_{f_{\star}}
, for
f_{\star}>0
sufficiently small.
∎

Remark 6.10
.

Note that in general,
\phi|_{\mathscr{D}}
in Theorem
6.9
is a conformal isometry of
\mathfrak{g}
.
Furthermore, the extra condition (
6.9
) implies that
\phi|_{\mathscr{D}}
is a full isometry of
\mathfrak{g}
.

Theorem
6.9
implies the following extension result for Killing vector fields, which is, in addition, the precise analogue of Theorem
1.7
from the introduction:

Theorem 6.11
.

Let
n>2
, and let
(\mathscr{M},g)
be a vacuum FG-aAdS segment, with holographic data
(\mathscr{I},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
.
Fix also a smooth vector field
\mathfrak{K}
on
\bar{\mathscr{D}}
,
55
55

55

More precisely,
\mathfrak{K}
is a vector field on some open
\mathscr{J}\subseteq\mathscr{I}
that contains
\bar{\mathscr{D}}
.
with
\mathscr{D}\subset\mathscr{I}
and
\bar{\mathscr{D}}
compact, and assume:

•

(\mathscr{M},g)
is regular to some large enough order
M_{0}
(depending on
\mathsf{g}
,
\mathscr{D}
).

•

(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
is gauge-equivalent on
\mathscr{D}
to some
(\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})
satisfying

(6.11)

(\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{K}}\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{K}}\check{\mathfrak{g}}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})|_{\mathscr{D}}=0\text{.}

•

(\mathscr{D},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)})
satisfies the GNCC.

Then,
\mathfrak{K}
extends to a Killing vector field
K
for
g
near
\{0\}\times\mathscr{D}
(viewed as part of the conformal boundary).
More precisely, there exist a sufficiently small
f_{\star}>0
and vector field

(6.12)

K:=K^{\rho}\partial_{\rho}+\mathsf{K}\text{,}

where
K^{\rho}\in C^{\infty}(\Omega_{f_{\star}})
and
\mathsf{K}
is a vertical vector field on
\Omega_{f_{\star}}
, such that
56
56

56

In other words,
K
is a
g
-Killing vector field that asymptotes to
\mathfrak{K}
at the conformal boundary.

(6.13)

\mathscr{L}_{K}g=0\text{,}\qquad K^{\rho}\rightarrow^{0}0\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{K}\rightarrow^{0}\mathfrak{K}\text{.}

Furthermore, if
\mathfrak{K}
is a
holographic Killing field
on
\mathscr{D}
, i.e.,

(6.14)

(\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{K}}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathscr{L}_{\mathfrak{K}}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})|_{\mathscr{D}}=0\text{.}

then
K
is vertical (
K^{\rho}\equiv 0
) and can be explicitly described via the relation

(6.15)

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{K}=0\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{K}|_{\sigma}\rightarrow^{0}\mathfrak{K}\text{.}

Proof.

Once again, by a gauge transformation, it suffices to consider the special case in which (
6.14
) holds.
Let
\phi_{s}
, for
s\in\mathbb{R}
small enough, denote transport along the integral curves of
\mathfrak{K}
by parameter
s
.
By definition, each
\phi_{s}
is a holographic isometry on
\mathscr{D}
,

(\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})|_{\mathscr{D}}=(\phi_{s\ast}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(0)},\phi_{s\ast}\mathfrak{g}^{\scriptscriptstyle(n)})|_{\mathscr{D}}\text{,}

so by Theorem
6.9
, it extends to a bulk isometry
\Phi_{s}
on some
\Omega_{f_{\star}}
,
f_{\star}>0
, with

(6.16)

\Phi_{s}(\sigma,p):=(\sigma,\phi_{s}(p))\text{,}

and with
\Phi_{s\ast}g=g
near
\{0\}\times\mathscr{D}
.
Let
K
be the generator of the family
\{\Phi_{s}\}_{s\in\mathbb{R}}
; note that by definition,
K
satisfies the first part of (
6.13
).
Finally, one directly deduces from (
6.16
) that
K
must be vertical, and that
K:=\mathsf{K}
satisfies the transport relation (
6.15
).
∎

7.
Additional Details and Computations

This appendix contains additional proofs and computational details for readers’ convenience.
We begin with some preliminary formulas that will be useful in later sections.

7.1.
Proof of Proposition
2.33

Throughout, we assume indices are with respect to arbitrary coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
, and we let
(k,l)
be the rank of
\mathsf{A}
.
The first part of (
2.33
) is a consequence of (
2.32
) and the following commutation formula from
[
50
, Proposition 2.27]
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}(\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{de}+\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{dc}-\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{ce})\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{g}^{de}(\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{db_{j}}+\mathsf{D}_{b_{j}}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{dc}-\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cb_{j}})\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]}\text{,}

Next, recalling the second formula in (
2.20
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{cd}\,\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{de}\,\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{b_{j}d}\,\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]}\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{D}_{c}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{de}\,\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}})-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}_{b_{j}d}\,\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]})\text{.}

Subtracting the above two equations and recalling the first part of (
2.33
) yields

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{cd}\,\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{m}_{de}\,\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{m}_{b_{j}d}\,\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{cd}\,\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{g}^{a_{i}d}(\mathsf{D}_{e}\mathsf{m}_{cd}-\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathsf{m}_{ce})\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![e]}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{g}^{de}(\mathsf{D}_{b_{j}}\mathsf{m}_{cd}-\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathsf{m}_{cb_{j}})\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![e]}\text{,}

from which the second identity in (
2.33
) follows.

Next, recalling Definition
2.27
for
\bar{\Box}
, we expand (partially in
\varphi_{\rho}
-coordinates)

(7.1)

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}(\rho^{p}\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{g}^{\alpha\beta}\bar{\nabla}_{\alpha}(\rho^{p}\bar{\nabla}_{\beta}\mathsf{A}+p\rho^{p-1}\bar{\nabla}_{\beta}\rho\cdot\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\rho^{p}\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}+2p\rho^{p-1}g^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\alpha}\rho\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\beta}\mathsf{A}+p(p-1)\rho^{p-2}g^{\alpha\beta}\nabla_{\alpha}\rho\nabla_{\beta}\rho\,\mathsf{A}+p\rho^{p-1}\Box\rho\,\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\rho^{p}\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}+2p\rho^{p+1}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}+p(p-1)\rho^{p}\,\mathsf{A}+p\rho^{p-1}\Box\rho\,\mathsf{A}\text{,}

where we also recalled (
2.7
) in the last step.
By (
2.7
), (
2.28
), and (
2.32
), we have

\displaystyle\Box\rho

\displaystyle=-\rho^{2}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}-\rho^{2}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\Gamma^{\rho}_{ab}

\displaystyle=-(n-1)\rho+\frac{1}{2}\rho^{2}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\mathsf{m}_{ab}\text{.}

Thus, combining (
7.1
) with the above yields

\bar{\Box}(\rho^{p}\mathsf{A})=\rho^{p}\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}+2p\rho^{p+1}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}-p(n-p)\rho^{p}\,\mathsf{A}+\frac{1}{2}p\rho^{p+1}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\mathsf{m}_{ab}\,\mathsf{A}\text{,}

which immediately imples (
2.34
).

Finally, for (
2.35
), the definitions of
\bar{\nabla}^{2}_{\rho\rho}
and
\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}^{2}
, along with Proposition
2.29
, yield:

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}})-\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{a_{i}}_{\rho d}\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![d]}{}_{\bar{b}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{d}_{\rho b_{j}}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![d]}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\bar{b}})+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{a_{i}}_{\rho d}\mathsf{A}^{\hat{a}_{i}\![d]}{}_{\bar{b}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{d}_{\rho b_{j}}\mathsf{A}^{\bar{a}}{}_{\hat{b}_{j}\![d]}\text{.}

Subtracting the above two equations and recalling the first part of (
2.28
) yields

\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}\mathsf{A}=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})+\rho^{-1}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}\text{.}

Furthermore, relating
\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}
using (
2.20
), the above then becomes
57
57

57

Here, we also used the standard identity
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}^{-1}=\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m})
.

(7.2)

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})+\rho^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}+\rho^{-1}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{2}\mathsf{A}+\rho^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})+\rho^{-1}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})\text{.}

Next, a similar decomposition of mixed and vertical derivatives in vertical components yields

\displaystyle\bar{\nabla}_{ab}\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{ab}\mathsf{A}-\Gamma^{\rho}_{ab}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{ab}\mathsf{A}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ab}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})_{ab}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{ab}\mathsf{A}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ab}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ab}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})_{ab}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})_{ab}\text{.}

Therefore, applying (
2.7
), Definition
2.27
, (
7.2
), and the above, we conclude that

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}\mathsf{A}+\rho^{2}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\bar{\nabla}_{ab}\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{2}\mathsf{A}-(n-1)\rho\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A}+\rho^{2}\mathsf{g}^{ab}\mathsf{D}_{ab}\mathsf{A}+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{A})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{A})\text{,}

which is precisely the last part of (
2.35
).

7.2.
Proof of Proposition
2.34

We assume all indices are with respect to
\varphi
- and
\varphi_{\rho}
-coordinates, and we let
\Gamma
and
\mathsf{\Gamma}
be the corresponding Christoffel symbols, as defined in Proposition
2.29
.
For future convenience, we also set
l:=l_{1}+l_{2}
, and we define (via indices) the vertical tensor fields

(7.3)

\mathsf{k}_{ac}:=\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ac}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{m}_{ac}\text{,}\qquad\mathsf{k}^{b}{}_{a}:=\rho^{-1}\delta^{b}{}_{a}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\mathsf{m}_{ac}\text{.}

By the definitions of
\nabla
and
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
(see Proposition
2.22
and (
2.29
)), along with (
2.28
), we have

\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}=\partial_{\rho}(F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}=\partial_{\rho}(\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}})-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\mathsf{f}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{.}

Subtracting the two equations above and applying (
2.28
) and (
2.36
) yields

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}(\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}-\mathsf{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}})\mathsf{f}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}+l_{1}\rho^{-1}\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}+l_{2}\rho^{-1}\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}+(l_{1}+l_{2})\rho^{-1}\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}\text{,}

from which the first equation in (
2.37
) follows.

Similarly, the definitions of
\nabla
and
\bar{\mathsf{D}}
imply

\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}=\partial_{c}(F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\Gamma_{c\rho}^{b}\,F_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![b]\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\Gamma_{ca_{j}}^{\beta}\,F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\beta]}\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}=\partial_{c}(\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}})-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{\Gamma}_{ca_{j}}^{b}\,\mathsf{f}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{.}

Subtracting the above equations and recalling (
2.28
), (
2.38
), and (
2.39
), we obtain

(7.4)

\displaystyle\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\Gamma_{c\rho}^{b}\,F_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![b]\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\Gamma_{ca_{j}}^{\rho}\,F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\rho]}

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{b}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{b\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{ca_{j}}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}\text{.}

Combining the above with (
7.3
) yields the second part of (
2.37
).

For the last identity in (
2.37
), we begin with
\rho
-derivatives.
First, by Proposition
2.29
,

(7.5)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rho\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})-\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{,}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}(\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})+(l_{1}+1)\rho^{-1}\,\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,\nabla_{\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{,}

Similarly, for the corresponding mixed derivatives, we apply (
2.29
) and compute

(7.6)

\displaystyle\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\partial_{\rho}[\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}]-\Gamma^{\rho}_{\rho\rho}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\rho}[\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}]+(l+1)\rho^{-1}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}\text{,}

where we also recalled (
2.28
) and the basic properties from Proposition
2.26
.
Subtracting (
7.6
) from (
7.5
), while applying both (
2.28
) and the first part of (
2.37
), we obtain that

(7.7)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\rho\rho}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}+(l_{1}-l)\rho^{-1}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}(\Gamma^{b}_{\rho a_{j}}-\mathsf{\Gamma}^{b}_{\rho a_{j}})\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\hat{a}_{j}\![b]}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}+(l_{1}-l)\rho^{-1}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}-l_{2}\rho^{-1}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}\text{.}

Next, applying again (
2.29
), we compute

\displaystyle\nabla_{bc}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{b}(\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}})-\Gamma^{\alpha}_{bc}\,\nabla_{\alpha}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\Gamma^{d}_{b\rho}\,\nabla_{c}F_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![d]\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\Gamma^{\delta}_{ba_{j}}\,\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\delta]}\text{,}

\displaystyle\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{b}(\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}})-\Gamma^{\alpha}_{bc}\,\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\alpha}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{d}_{ba_{j}}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![d]}\text{.}

Subtracting the two equations and recalling (
2.28
) then yields

(7.8)

\displaystyle\nabla_{bc}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}+\partial_{b}(\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}})-\Gamma^{\alpha}_{bc}[\nabla_{\alpha}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\rho^{-l}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\alpha}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}]

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\Gamma^{d}_{b\rho}\,\nabla_{c}F_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![d]\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\Gamma^{\rho}_{ba_{j}}\,\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\rho]}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{\Gamma}^{d}_{ba_{j}}\,(\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}})

\displaystyle:=\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}+I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{3}+I_{4}+I_{5}\text{.}

To simplify the upcoming computations, we define, for all
1\leq i\leq l_{1}
and
1\leq j\leq l_{2}
, the vertical tensor fields
\mathsf{z}
,
\mathsf{z}^{\rho}_{i}
,
\mathsf{z}^{v}_{j}
—of ranks
(0,l_{2}+1)
,
(0,l_{2}+2)
,
(0,l_{2})
, respectively—via the index formulas

(7.9)

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}_{c\bar{a}}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{z}^{\rho}_{i})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{c}F_{\hat{\rho}_{i}\![b]\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{b\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{z}^{v}_{j})_{c\hat{a}_{j}}

\displaystyle:=\nabla_{c}F_{\bar{\rho}\hat{a}_{j}\![\rho]}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}\text{.}

Applying (
2.28
), the first part of (
2.37
), and (
7.9
) to the term
I_{2}
from (
7.8
), we obtain

I_{2}=-\Gamma^{d}_{bc}(\nabla_{d}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}-\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{d}\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}})\text{.}

From (
7.8
), the first part of (
7.9
), and the above, we see that

(7.10)

I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{5}=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}\mathsf{z}_{c\bar{a}}\text{.}

Similarly, for
I_{3}
and
I_{4}
, we again apply (
2.28
) and (
7.9
):

(7.11)

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{d\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\,(\mathsf{z}^{\rho}_{i})_{cd\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\,(\mathsf{z}^{v}_{j})_{c\hat{a}_{j}}\text{.}

Now, recalling (
7.4
), along with (
7.9
), we deduce

(7.12)

\displaystyle\mathsf{z}_{c\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{e\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{z}^{\rho}_{i})_{cd\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq j\leq l_{1}\\
j\neq i\end{subarray}}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{ed\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{d\hat{a}_{j}}-\mathsf{k}_{bc}\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle(\mathsf{z}^{v}_{j})_{c\hat{a}_{j}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{e\hat{a}_{j}}+\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,\mathsf{f}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![e]}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq i\leq l_{2}\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{i}c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}\text{.}

Combining (
7.3
), (
7.10
), and the above, we conclude that

(7.13)

\displaystyle I_{1}+I_{2}+I_{5}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}[\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{e\bar{a}}+\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{e\bar{a}}]-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}[\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}+\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}]

\displaystyle=\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{c\bar{a}}-\rho^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}c}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}f^{\rho}_{i})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{cb\bar{a}}\text{.}

Similar computations using (
7.11
) also yield

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{d\bar{a}}+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{ed\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{d\hat{a}_{j}}-l_{1}\mathsf{k}^{d}{}_{b}\mathsf{k}_{dc}\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{b\bar{a}}-l_{1}\rho^{-2}\mathsf{g}_{bc}\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}+2\rho^{-2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}-\rho^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{b\hat{a}_{j}}\text{,}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{cb\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f})_{cb\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}\text{,}

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{e\hat{a}_{j}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\mathsf{k}^{e}{}_{c}\,\mathsf{f}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![e]}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathsf{k}_{a_{i}c}\mathsf{k}_{a_{j}b}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}

\displaystyle=-\rho^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}b}\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{c}(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-\rho^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}b}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{c\hat{a}_{j}}-\rho^{-2}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}b}\,\mathsf{f}_{\hat{a}_{j}\![c]}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\rho^{-2}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{i}c}\mathsf{g}_{a_{j}b}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}+\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{cb\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f})_{cb\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}\text{.}

Finally, combining (
7.8
), (
7.13
), and the above, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathsf{g}^{bc}\,\nabla_{bc}F_{\bar{\rho}\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-l}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}+2\rho^{-1}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{b}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho}_{i})_{c\bar{a}}-\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{a_{j}}(\mathsf{f}^{v}_{j})_{\hat{a}_{j}}-(nl_{1}+l_{2})\rho^{-2}\,\mathsf{f}_{\bar{a}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+2\rho^{-2}\left[\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathsf{g}^{bc}\,(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{cb\bar{a}}-\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}(\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{a_{j}\hat{a}_{j}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathsf{g}_{a_{i}a_{j}}\,(\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\hat{a}_{i,j}}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}f^{\rho}_{i})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\bar{\mathsf{D}}f^{v}_{j})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},f^{\rho}_{i})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},f^{v}_{j})_{\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{1}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-3},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,\rho}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{-1}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{v,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}+\rho^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{i=1}^{l_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{l_{2}}\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{f}^{\rho,v}_{i,j})_{\bar{a}}\text{.}

The last formula in (
2.37
) now follows from (
7.7
), the above, and the fact that

\Box F=\rho^{2}(\nabla_{\rho\rho}F+\mathsf{g}^{bc}\nabla_{bc}F)\text{,}\qquad\rho^{-1}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})=\rho^{2}[\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{\rho\rho}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})+\mathsf{g}^{bc}\rho^{-l}\bar{\nabla}_{bc}(\rho^{l}\mathsf{f})]\text{.}

7.3.
Proof of Proposition
3.4

Throughout this proof, we assume all indices are raised and lowered using
g
.
First, the Bianchi identity and Proposition
2.13
yield

(7.14)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu}W_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}+\nabla_{\gamma}W_{\alpha\beta\delta\mu}+\nabla_{\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\text{,}

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}W_{\mu\beta\gamma\delta}=\nabla^{\mu}W_{\mu\beta\gamma\delta}+\nabla_{\gamma}W_{\mu\beta\delta}{}^{\mu}+\nabla_{\delta}W_{\mu\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=0\text{,}

which immediately proves the first identity in (
3.4
).

Next, taking a divergence of the first part of (
7.14
) (in the “
\mu
” component) yields

(7.15)

0=\Box\mathcal{W}_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}+\nabla^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\alpha\beta\delta\mu}+\nabla^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\mu\gamma}\text{.}

Commuting derivatives and then applying the second part of (
7.14
), we obtain

(7.16)

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\alpha\beta\delta\mu}

\displaystyle=-R^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\lambda\beta\delta\mu}-R^{\lambda}{}_{\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\alpha\lambda\delta\mu}-R^{\lambda}{}_{\delta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\alpha\beta\lambda\mu}-R^{\lambda}{}_{\mu}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\alpha\beta\delta\lambda}

\displaystyle:=I_{1,1}+I_{1,2}+I_{1,3}+I_{1,4}\text{,}

\displaystyle\nabla^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle=-R^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\lambda\beta\mu\gamma}-R^{\lambda}{}_{\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\alpha\lambda\mu\gamma}-R^{\lambda}{}_{\mu}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\lambda\gamma}-R^{\lambda}{}_{\gamma}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\mu\lambda}

\displaystyle:=I_{2,1}+I_{2,2}+I_{2,3}+I_{2,4}\text{.}

Now, using Proposition
2.13
, we expand

\displaystyle I_{1,1}+I_{2,2}

\displaystyle=-(W^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}+g^{\lambda}{}_{\gamma}g_{\alpha}{}^{\mu})W_{\lambda\beta\delta\mu}-(W^{\lambda}{}_{\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}+g^{\lambda}{}_{\delta}g_{\beta}{}^{\mu})W_{\alpha\lambda\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle=-2W_{\alpha\delta\beta\gamma}+W^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\lambda\beta\mu\delta}+W^{\lambda}{}_{\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\lambda\alpha\mu\gamma}\text{,}

\displaystyle I_{1,2}+I_{2,1}

\displaystyle=-(W^{\lambda}{}_{\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}+g^{\lambda}{}_{\gamma}g_{\beta}{}^{\mu})W_{\alpha\lambda\delta\mu}-(W^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}+g^{\lambda}{}_{\delta}g_{\alpha}{}^{\mu})W_{\lambda\beta\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle=-2W_{\alpha\gamma\delta\beta}-W^{\lambda}{}_{\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\lambda\alpha\mu\delta}-W^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\lambda\beta\mu\gamma}\text{,}

\displaystyle I_{1,3}+I_{2,4}

\displaystyle=-(W^{\lambda}{}_{\delta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}+g^{\lambda}{}_{\gamma}g_{\delta}{}^{\mu})W_{\alpha\beta\lambda\mu}-(W^{\lambda}{}_{\gamma}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}+g^{\lambda}{}_{\delta}g_{\gamma}{}^{\mu})W_{\alpha\beta\mu\lambda}

\displaystyle=-2W_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}-W^{\lambda}{}_{\delta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\alpha\beta\lambda\mu}-W^{\lambda}{}_{\gamma}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\mu\lambda}\text{,}

\displaystyle I_{1,4}+I_{2,3}

\displaystyle=2nW_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}\text{.}

Summing the above and recalling the symmetries of
W
, we then obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{j=1}^{4}I_{i,j}

\displaystyle=2nW_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}+W^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\lambda\beta\mu\delta}+W^{\lambda}{}_{\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\lambda\alpha\mu\gamma}

\displaystyle\qquad-W^{\lambda}{}_{\beta}{}^{\mu}{}_{\gamma}W_{\lambda\alpha\mu\delta}-W^{\lambda}{}_{\alpha}{}^{\mu}{}_{\delta}W_{\lambda\beta\mu\gamma}+W^{\lambda\mu}{}_{\gamma\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\lambda\mu}\text{.}

Combining (
7.15
), (
7.16
), and the above results in the remaining equation of (
3.4
).

7.4.
Proof of Proposition
3.5

Let us begin with the first two identities in (
3.5
).
Applying the first equation in (
2.37
) to
F:=W
(with permuted indices) yields

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\nabla_{\rho}W_{\rho a\rho b}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab})

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}\text{,}

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\nabla_{\rho}W_{\rho abc}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2}\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc})

\displaystyle=\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}\text{.}

Applying (
2.7
) and the first identity in (
3.4
) to the left-hand sides of the above yields

(7.17)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{g}^{cd}\nabla_{c}(\rho^{2}W)_{\rho bad}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{g}^{de}\nabla_{d}(\rho^{2}W)_{eabc}\text{.}

Applying the second identity of (
2.37
) to the first equation in (
7.17
), we have

(7.18)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bad}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}^{cd}\,\mathsf{w}^{0}_{dacb}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathsf{g}_{cd}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathsf{g}_{ca}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{db}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})_{ab}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})_{ab}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bad}+n\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})_{ab}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})_{ab}\text{,}

where in the last step, we also used Definition
3.1
and the trace-free property of
W
to obtain

-\mathsf{g}^{cd}\,\mathsf{w}^{0}_{dacb}=\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}\text{.}

The first part of (
3.5
) follows immediately from (
7.18
).

Similarly, applying (
2.37
) to the second part of (
7.17
) yields

(7.19)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{eabc}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{g}_{de}\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{g}_{da}\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{ebc}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{g}_{db}\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{cea}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{g}_{dc}\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bea}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})_{abc}

\displaystyle=-\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{eabc}+n\rho^{-1}\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})_{abc}\text{,}

where in the last step, we noted from Definition
3.1
and the symmetries of
W
that

-\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\mathsf{w}^{1}_{cba}-\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bca}=0\text{.}

The second identity in (
3.5
) now follows from (
7.19
).

For the two remaining parts of (
3.5
), we again start with the first part of (
2.37
):

\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}=\rho^{2}\nabla_{\rho}W_{\rho abc}\text{,}\qquad\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}=\rho^{2}\nabla_{\rho}W_{abcd}\text{.}

The right-hand sides of the above can then be expanded using the Bianchi identity for
W
:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{b}(\rho^{2}W)_{\rho a\rho c}-\nabla_{c}(\rho^{2}W)_{\rho a\rho b}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{a}(\rho^{2}W)_{\rho bcd}-\nabla_{b}(\rho^{2}W)_{\rho acd}\text{.}

Applying (
2.37
) to each term on the right-hand side of the above, we then obtain

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{cba}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}-\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bca}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{acb}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})_{abc}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac}-\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+3\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})_{abc}\text{,}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{a}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bcd}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ac}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bd}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ad}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bc}-\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{acd}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{bacd}

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{bc}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ad}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{bd}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})_{abcd}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{a}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bcd}-\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{acd}+2\rho^{-1}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ad}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bc}+\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{bc}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ad}

\displaystyle\qquad-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{ac}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bd}-\rho^{-1}\mathsf{g}_{bd}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})_{abcd}+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})_{abcd}\text{.}

The last two identities of (
3.5
) are now immediate consequences of the above.

7.5.
Proof of Proposition
3.6

Throughout, we assume all indices are with respect to
\varphi
and
\varphi_{\rho}
, for some arbitrary coordinates
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
.
Also, for convenience, we define
Q
by

(7.20)

Q_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}:=g^{\lambda\kappa}g^{\mu\nu}(2W_{\lambda\alpha\mu\delta}W_{\kappa\beta\nu\gamma}-2W_{\lambda\alpha\mu\gamma}W_{\kappa\beta\nu\delta}-W_{\lambda\mu\gamma\delta}W_{\alpha\beta\kappa\nu})\text{,}

that is, the right-hand side of the wave equation in (
3.4
).
Then, by (
2.7
) and (
3.1
), we have

(7.21)

\displaystyle Q_{\rho b\rho d}

\displaystyle=g^{\lambda\kappa}g^{\mu\nu}(2W_{\lambda\rho\mu d}W_{\kappa b\nu\rho}-2W_{\lambda\rho\mu\rho}W_{\kappa b\nu d}-W_{\lambda\mu\rho d}W_{\rho b\kappa\nu})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{w}^{2},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{0},\mathsf{w}^{2})\text{,}

\displaystyle Q_{\rho bcd}

\displaystyle=g^{\lambda\kappa}g^{\mu\nu}(2W_{\lambda\rho\mu d}W_{\kappa b\nu c}-2W_{\lambda\rho\mu c}W_{\kappa b\nu d}-W_{\lambda\mu cd}W_{\rho b\kappa\nu})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{0},\mathsf{w}^{1})\text{,}

\displaystyle Q_{abcd}

\displaystyle=g^{\lambda\kappa}g^{\mu\nu}(2W_{\lambda a\mu d}W_{\kappa b\nu c}-2W_{\lambda a\mu c}W_{\kappa b\nu d}-W_{\lambda\mu cd}W_{ab\kappa\nu})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{w}^{2},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{w}^{1},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{w}^{0},\mathsf{w}^{0})\text{.}

We now apply (
3.1
) and the last equation in (
2.37
) to
W
to obtain

(7.22)

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\Box W_{\rho a\rho b}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{2})_{ab}+2\rho\mathsf{g}^{cd}(\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bda}+\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{adb})-0-(2n+2)\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}

\displaystyle\qquad+4\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+2\mathsf{g}^{cd}\,\mathsf{w}^{0}_{cadb}+0+\mathsf{E}^{2}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{2})_{ab}+2\rho\mathsf{g}^{cd}(\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bda}+\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{adb})-2n\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+\mathsf{E}^{2}_{ab}\text{,}

where we used that
-\mathsf{w}^{2}
is the
\mathsf{g}
-trace of
\mathsf{w}^{0}
, and where the error terms
\mathsf{E}^{2}
are given by

(7.23)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}^{2}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-3},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})\text{,}

Applying the second part of (
3.4
) and the first part of (
3.5
) to (
7.22
) yields

\displaystyle\rho^{-2}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{2})_{ab}+4(n-2)\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\Box W_{\rho a\rho b}+4\rho\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+2n\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+\mathsf{E}^{2}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}Q_{\rho a\rho b}+4\rho\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+\mathsf{E}^{2}_{ab}\text{,}

Moreover, expanding the left-hand side using (
2.34
) (with
p=2
), we see that

(7.24)

\bar{\Box}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}+2(n-2)\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}=\mathsf{E}^{2}_{ab}+\rho^{2}Q_{\rho a\rho b}\text{,}

The first equation in (
3.6
) now follows from (
7.21
), (
7.23
), and the above.

The remaining parts of (
3.6
) are treated similarly.
Again, we first apply the last part of (
2.37
):

(7.25)

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\Box W_{\rho abc}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{1})_{abc}+2\rho\mathsf{g}^{de}\,\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{eabc}+2\rho\,(\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ab}-\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac})

\displaystyle\qquad-(n+3)\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\mathsf{E}^{1}_{abc}\text{,}

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\Box W_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{0})_{abcd}-2\rho\,(\mathsf{D}_{a}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{bcd}-\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{acd}+\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{dab}-\mathsf{D}_{d}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{cab})

\displaystyle\qquad-4\,\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}+2(\mathsf{g}_{ac}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bd}-\mathsf{g}_{ad}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bc}-\mathsf{g}_{bc}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ad}+\mathsf{g}_{bd}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac})+\mathsf{E}^{0}_{abcd}\text{,}

where we also made use of the symmetries of
W
, and where the corresponding error terms are

(7.26)

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}^{1}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{0})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}^{0}

\displaystyle=\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{D}\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{1})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-2},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{0})+\rho\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g},\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})+\rho^{2}\,\mathscr{S}(\mathsf{g}^{-1},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{m},\mathsf{w}^{2})\text{.}

The first-order terms in the right-hand sides of (
7.25
) can be further expanded using (
3.5
), and the terms involving
\bar{\Box}
in (
7.27
) can be expanded using (
2.34
):

(7.27)

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\Box W_{\rho abc}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{1})_{abc}-4\rho\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+(n-5)\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\mathsf{E}^{1}_{abc}

\displaystyle=\bar{\Box}\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}-(n+1)\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\mathsf{E}^{1}_{abc}\text{,}

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\Box W_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\rho^{-2}\bar{\Box}(\rho^{2}\mathsf{w}^{0})_{abcd}-4\rho\,\bar{\mathsf{D}}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}-4\,\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}

\displaystyle\qquad+2(\mathsf{g}_{ad}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bc}+\mathsf{g}_{bc}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ad}-\mathsf{g}_{ac}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bd}-\mathsf{g}_{bd}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac})+\mathsf{E}^{0}_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\bar{\Box}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}-2n\,\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}+2(\mathsf{g}_{ad}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bc}+\mathsf{g}_{bc}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ad}-\mathsf{g}_{ac}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bd}-\mathsf{g}_{bd}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac})+\mathsf{E}^{0}_{abcd}\text{.}

The left-hand sides of (
7.27
) can be expanded using (
3.4
), (
7.20
), and (
7.21
):

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\Box W_{\rho abc}

\displaystyle=-2n\,\mathsf{w}^{1}_{abc}+\rho^{2}\,Q_{\rho abc}\text{,}

\displaystyle\rho^{2}\Box W_{abcd}

\displaystyle=-2n\,\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}+\rho^{2}\,Q_{abcd}\text{.}

Combining the above with (
7.21
) and (
7.27
) results in the second equation in (
3.6
), as well as

\bar{\Box}\mathsf{w}^{0}_{abcd}=-2(\mathsf{g}_{ad}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bc}+\mathsf{g}_{bc}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ad}-\mathsf{g}_{ac}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{bd}-\mathsf{g}_{bd}\,\mathsf{w}^{2}_{ac})+\mathsf{E}^{0}_{abcd}+\rho^{2}Q_{abcd}\text{.}

Recalling (
3.2
), (
7.24
), and the above, we then see that

\displaystyle\bar{\Box}\mathsf{w}^{\star}_{abcd}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{E}^{0}_{abcd}-\frac{1}{n-2}(\mathsf{g}_{ad}\mathsf{E}^{2}_{bc}+\mathsf{g}_{bc}\mathsf{E}^{2}_{ad}-\mathsf{g}_{ac}\mathsf{E}^{2}_{bd}-\mathsf{g}_{bd}\mathsf{E}_{ac})+\rho^{2}Q_{abcd}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{\rho^{2}}{n-2}(\mathsf{g}_{ad}Q_{\rho b\rho c}+\mathsf{g}_{bc}Q_{\rho a\rho d}-\mathsf{g}_{ac}Q_{\rho b\rho d}-\mathsf{g}_{bd}Q_{\rho a\rho c})\text{.}

The last equation in (
3.6
) now follows from (
7.21
), (
7.23
), (
7.26
), and the above.

7.6.
Proof of Proposition
3.8

The first two parts of (
3.8
) follow immediately from Theorem
2.16
, in particular from the boundary limits for
\mathsf{g}
and
\mathsf{g}^{-1}
.
The last two parts of (
3.8
) also follow from Theorem
2.16
, using the boundary limits for
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}^{2}\mathsf{g}
, along with Taylor’s theorem.

Next, for the Weyl curvature components, the key is the formulation (
3.3
) of the Einstein-vacuum equations.
From the boundary limits in Theorem
2.16
, we see that

(7.28)

\rho^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)\text{,}\qquad-\mathscr{L}^{2}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}+\rho^{-1}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{g}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho)\text{.}

Moreover, writing the components of
\mathsf{R}
in terms of coordinate derivatives of
\mathsf{g}
yields

(7.29)

\mathsf{R}=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(1)\text{.}

Combining (
3.3
), (
3.8
), (
7.28
), and (
7.29
) results in the asymptotics for
\mathsf{w}^{0}
,
\mathsf{w}^{1}
, and
\mathsf{w}^{2}
in (
3.9
).
The asymptotics for
\mathsf{w}^{\star}
in (
3.9
) then follow from (
3.2
) and the above.

The asymptotics for
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}
,
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}
, and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}
are proved similarly—we apply
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}
to both sides of (
3.3
), and we then apply the boundary limits in Theorem
2.16
to each term on the right-hand side.
In addition, we make use of the following observations:

•

For
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{1}
, we also use (
2.33
) to commute
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}
with
\mathsf{D}
.

•

For
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}
, we express the components of
\mathsf{R}
in terms of derivatives of
\mathsf{g}
, which yields

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{R}=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1)\text{.}

•

For both
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{2}
, we also note that

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\rho^{-1}\mathsf{m})=\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(1)\text{.}

Finally, the asymptotics for
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{w}^{\star}
once again follow from (
3.2
) and the above.

7.7.
Proof of Proposition
3.12

The first part of (
3.14
) is a consequence of the following identity, which holds with respect to any coordinate system
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
:

\mathsf{g}^{ab}-\check{\mathsf{g}}^{ab}=-\mathsf{g}^{ac}(\mathsf{g}_{cd}-\check{\mathsf{g}}_{cd})\check{\mathsf{g}}^{bd}\text{,}

Next, we can view (
3.11
) as a system of differential equations for the components of
\mathsf{Q}
, with coefficients and sources given by
\mathsf{g}^{-1}
,
\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}
, and
\mathsf{m}
.
By solving these equations, we can write the components of
\mathsf{Q}|_{\sigma}
,
\sigma\in(0,\rho_{0}]
, as integrals in
\rho
(from
0
to
\sigma
) of some combination of
\mathsf{g}^{-1}
,
\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}
, and
\mathsf{m}
, along with a matrix exponential factor.
Combining the above with (
3.8
) results in the second part of (
3.14
).
(In particular, we obtain one extra power of
\rho
from the integral with respect to
\rho
.)
The third part of (
3.14
) then follows from (
3.8
), (
3.12
), and the second part of (
3.14
).

It remains to prove (
3.15
).
The first part of (
3.15
) is immediate, since it is clear from the first two parts of (
2.28
) that
\smash{\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\rho\rho},\check{\Gamma}^{\alpha}_{\rho\rho}}
and
\smash{\Gamma^{\rho}_{\alpha\rho},\check{\Gamma}^{\rho}_{\alpha\rho}}
are identical.
Moreover, the second identity in (
3.15
) follows immediately from applying the fifth part of (
2.28
).
Similarly, for the third part of (
3.15
), we use the third and fourth parts of (
2.28
) in order to obtain

(\Gamma-\check{\Gamma})^{a}_{\rho b}=(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{a}_{\rho b}=\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathsf{m}_{ad}-\check{\mathsf{g}}^{cd}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ad})\text{.}

Recalling the asymptotics of (
3.8
) and the first part of (
3.14
) yields, as desired,

\displaystyle(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{a}_{\rho b}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}(\mathsf{g}^{cd}-\check{\mathsf{g}}^{cd})\mathsf{m}_{ad}+\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{cd}(\mathsf{m}_{ad}-\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ad})

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}^{-1}-\check{\mathsf{g}}^{-1})^{a}{}_{b}+\mathscr{O}_{M}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})^{a}{}_{b}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})^{a}{}_{b}+\mathscr{O}_{M}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})^{a}{}_{b}\text{.}

For the last part of (
3.15
), note the last part of (
2.28
) implies

(\Gamma-\check{\Gamma})^{c}_{ab}=(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{c}_{ab}\text{.}

Expanding the Christoffel symbols, we then obtain, via a direct computation,

\displaystyle(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{c}_{ab}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{cd}(\partial_{a}\mathsf{g}_{db}+\partial_{b}\mathsf{g}_{da}-\partial_{d}\mathsf{g}_{ab})-\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{cd}(\partial_{a}\check{\mathsf{g}}_{db}+\partial_{b}\check{\mathsf{g}}_{da}-\partial_{d}\check{\mathsf{g}}_{ab})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{cd}[\mathsf{D}_{a}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{db}+\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{da}-\mathsf{D}_{d}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ab}]\text{,}

as desired (see also
[
53
, Appendix D]
).
The final equality in (
3.15
) now follows from (
3.8
).

7.8.
Proof of Proposition
3.13

The first part of (
3.16
) follows from the definitions (
2.32
) of
\mathsf{m}
and
\check{\mathsf{m}}
.
For the second part of (
3.16
), we combine the asymptotics (
3.8
) with the definition (
3.11
).

Next, we take the difference of two applications of the third part of (
3.3
) to obtain

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{ab}=\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{ab}-2(\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2})_{ab}+\frac{1}{4}(\mathsf{g}^{cd}\mathsf{m}_{ad}\mathsf{m}_{bc}-\check{\mathsf{g}}^{cd}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ad}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{bc})\text{.}

The last term can be treated using (
3.8
) and the first part of (
3.14
), and we obtain

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})=\rho^{-1}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})-2(\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho^{2};\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})\text{.}

The third part of (
3.16
) follows immediately from the above, since (
3.9
) and (
3.13
) imply

\mathsf{w}^{2}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2}=\mathsf{W}^{2}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})\text{.}

For the final identity in (
3.16
), we begin by applying
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}
to (
3.12
), and then commuting
\mathsf{D}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\rho}
via (
2.33
).
The error term in (
2.33
) can then be expressed as asymptotic terms using (
3.8
):

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{B}_{cab}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ab}-\mathsf{D}_{a}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cb}-\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{Q}_{ca}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})_{cab}\text{.}

For the first three terms on the right-hand side, we apply (
2.32
), (
3.8
), and (
3.11
) to obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{B}_{cab}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{ab}-\mathsf{D}_{a}(\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{cb}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{D}_{b}[\mathsf{m}_{ad}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{ce}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{D}_{b}[\mathsf{m}_{cd}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{ae}]+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})_{cab}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{D}_{c}\mathsf{m}_{ab}-\mathsf{D}_{a}\mathsf{m}_{cb})-(\mathsf{D}_{c}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ab}-\mathsf{D}_{a}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{cb})-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{ad}\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{ce}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{cd}\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{ae}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})_{cab}\text{.}

Applying the first part of (
3.3
), we then have

(7.30)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{B}_{cab}

\displaystyle=2(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1})_{bac}-(\mathsf{D}-\check{\mathsf{D}})_{c}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ab}+(\mathsf{D}-\check{\mathsf{D}})_{a}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{cb}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{ad}\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{ce}

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{cd}\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{ae}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})_{cab}\text{.}

For the terms in (
7.30
) involving
\mathsf{D}-\check{\mathsf{D}}
, we expand using the last part of (
2.28
):

\displaystyle-(\mathsf{D}-\check{\mathsf{D}})_{c}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ab}+(\mathsf{D}-\check{\mathsf{D}})_{a}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{cb}

\displaystyle=(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{d}_{ca}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{db}+(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{d}_{cb}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ad}-(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{d}_{ac}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{db}-(\mathsf{\Gamma}-\check{\mathsf{\Gamma}})^{d}_{ab}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{cd}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{de}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ad}[\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{eb}+\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ec}-\mathsf{D}_{e}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{bc}]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{de}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{cd}[\mathsf{D}_{a}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{eb}+\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ea}-\mathsf{D}_{e}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ba}]\text{.}

Combining the above with (
7.30
), we conclude that

(7.31)

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{B}_{cab}

\displaystyle=2(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1})_{bac}+\frac{1}{2}(\check{\mathsf{g}}^{de}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ad}-\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{ad})\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ce}

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}(\check{\mathsf{g}}^{de}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{cd}-\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{cd})\mathsf{D}_{b}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ae}+\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{de}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{ad}[\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{eb}-\mathsf{D}_{e}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{bc}]

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{de}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{cd}[\mathsf{D}_{a}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{eb}-\mathsf{D}_{e}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ba}]-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{ad}\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{Q}_{ce}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{cd}\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{Q}_{ae}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})_{cab}

\displaystyle=2(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1})_{bac}+\mathcal{I}_{1}+\mathcal{I}_{2}+\mathcal{I}_{3}+\mathcal{I}_{4}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{ad}\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{Q}_{ce}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{cd}\mathsf{D}_{b}\mathsf{Q}_{ae}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})_{cab}\text{.}

For
\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}
, we take as “main terms” the differences
\mathsf{g}^{-1}-\check{\mathsf{g}}^{-1}
and
\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}}
.
(In particular, we treat
\mathsf{D}\mathsf{g}
and
\mathsf{D}\check{\mathsf{g}}
as coefficients of the form
\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1)
.)
Recalling (
3.8
) and (
3.14
) then yields

(7.32)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{cab}\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{cab}\text{.}

By similar computations, we also obtain

(7.33)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{ad}[\mathsf{D}_{c}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{eb}-\mathsf{D}_{e}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{bc}]+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{cab}\text{,}

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{4}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\check{\mathsf{g}}^{de}\check{\mathsf{m}}_{cd}[\mathsf{D}_{a}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{eb}-\mathsf{D}_{e}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{ba}]+\mathscr{O}_{M-2}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{cab}\text{.}

Combining (
7.31
)–(
7.33
) and recalling (
3.12
) results in

\displaystyle\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{B}_{cab}

\displaystyle=2(\mathsf{w}^{1}-\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1})_{bac}+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{ad}\mathsf{B}_{ceb}-\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{g}^{de}\mathsf{m}_{cd}\mathsf{B}_{aeb}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}})_{cab}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{Q})_{cab}+\mathscr{O}_{M-1}(1;\mathsf{m}-\check{\mathsf{m}})_{cab}\text{.}

The above, along with Proposition
3.8
and (
3.13
), immediately yield the last part of (
3.16
).

It remains to prove the derivative transport equations (
3.17
).
These are in fact immediate consequences of the preceding (
3.16
), since (
2.33
) and (
3.8
) imply that

\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A}=\mathsf{D}\mathscr{L}_{\rho}\mathsf{D}\mathsf{A}+\mathscr{O}_{M-3}(\rho;\mathsf{A})

holds for any vertical tensor field
\mathsf{A}
.

7.9.
Proof of Proposition
3.14

We prove all three formulas at once.
Throughout, we fix

(\mathsf{w},\check{\mathsf{w}},\mathsf{W})\in\{(\mathsf{w}^{2},\check{\mathsf{w}}^{2},\mathsf{W}^{2}),(\mathsf{w}^{1},\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1},\mathsf{W}^{1}),(\mathsf{w}^{\star},\check{\mathsf{w}}^{1},\mathsf{W}^{\star})\}\text{,}

as well as the corresponding constant
\sigma_{\mathsf{w}}
:

\sigma_{\mathsf{w}^{2}}:=2(n-2)\text{,}\qquad\sigma_{\mathsf{w}^{1}}:=n-1\text{,}\qquad\sigma_{\mathsf{w}^{\star}}:=0\text{.}

Then, from (
3.13
), we see that
\mathsf{W}
satisfies the following wave equation:

(7.34)

\displaystyle(\bar{\Box}+\sigma_{\mathsf{w}})\mathsf{W}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle=[(\bar{\Box}+\sigma_{\mathsf{w}})\mathsf{w}-(\check{\bar{\Box}}+\sigma_{\mathsf{w}})\check{\mathsf{w}}]_{\bar{a}}-(\bar{\Box}-\check{\bar{\Box}})\check{\mathsf{w}}_{\bar{a}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathsf{g}^{bc}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\bar{\Box}+\sigma_{\mathsf{w}})[\check{\mathsf{w}}_{\bar{a}_{j}[b]}(\mathsf{g}-\check{\mathsf{g}}+\mathsf{Q})_{a_{j}c}]

\displaystyle=\mathscr{A}_{0}+\mathscr{A}_{1}+\mathscr{A}_{2}\text{.}

Here and below, we index with respect to an arbitrary coordinate system
(U,\varphi)
on
\mathscr{I}
.

To expand
\mathscr{A}_{0}
, we use that both
\mathsf{w}
and
\check{\mathsf{w}}
solve wave equations of the form (
3.6
), and we take the difference of these two equations.
In particular, we take the difference of each pair of corresponding terms on the right-hand side of the relevant equation in (
3.6
).
